## 2026-06-03

### Behavior or Interface Changes

- **WP-VIS-1: blob_walk_v2 per-frame debug tile overlays now scale with the runner's torso box
  instead of fixed pixels, keeping geometry legible at every crop/zoom level (contract C2):
  `walk_draw.py`,
  `walk_render.py`.**
  Replaced hardcoded `size_px=12` (plus marker), `square_half=8` (audit square), and
  `thickness=1` (velocity vector, allowed-jump circle, audit square, residual line) with
  torso-height-derived values. New helpers `compute_plus_arm_px` and `compute_sq_half_px`
  in `walk_draw.py` use named, commented pixel-space min/max legibility clamp constants
  (the allowed C2 exception for non-scene quantities).

- **WP-VIS-2: blob_walk_v2 tile draw order is now data-driven via a new `walk_tile_layer_order`
  key in [overlay_styles.yaml](../track_runner/overlay_styles.yaml):
  `walk_render.py`,
  `walk_palette.py`.**
  `render_walk_tile` was restructured so each overlay (including the heat-map
  alpha-composite) is a named layer dispatched in the resolved order; the canvas now starts
  as the source crop rather than pre-compositing heat. Default order draws heat last
  (on top) so the motion-cue heat is no longer hidden behind boxes/markers. Heat content,
  threshold, and 0.40 alpha are unchanged.
  `walk_palette.resolve_layer_order()` behavior: missing key falls back to the built-in
  default; unknown, duplicate, or omitted layer names raise `ValueError` loudly.

- **WP-RPT-1: blob_walk_v2 per-interval summary now prints real numbers or a reason token
  instead of a bare "?" for `accepted_fraction`, `max`/`median actual_jump`,
  `final_disp_to_neighbor`, and `longest_no_accept_streak`:
  `walk_html.py`.**
  Undefined values print a naming token (`no_walkable_frames`, `no_accepted_position`,
  `no_neighbor_seed`, `no_jump_rows`); `longest_no_accept_streak` prints
  `no_walkable_frames` instead of a misleading 0 when undefined; the " W" unit is
  suppressed for reason tokens.

### Fixes and Maintenance

- **WP-RPT-1 root cause: `_compute_walk_quality` discarded every real walk row by filtering
  on a blank `step` field, so all quality metrics came back undefined:
  `walk_html.py`,
  [walk_walker.py](../track_runner/blob_walk/walk_walker.py).**
  The walker writes a blank `step` on every emitted walk row (only the bootstrap row
  carries an explicit step); the fix treats a blank step as a walk row and excludes only
  the explicit `step=="0"` bootstrap row. `walk_walker.py` now populates the pre-existing
  `actual_jump` column with the per-frame pred-to-cand Euclidean displacement (a recorded
  value only; no change to candidate scoring or acceptance). No `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump
  (`actual_jump` already existed at `SCHEMA_VERSION=13`; it is now merely populated,
  per contract C10).

### Developer Tests and Notes

- **Added `test_blob_walk_v2_layer_order.py`.**
  Fast unit tests for `walk_palette.resolve_layer_order` validation: missing-key default
  fallback, and unknown / duplicate / omitted layer names raise `ValueError`.

- **Added `test_blob_walk_v2_visible_seed_filter.py`.**
  Regression tests locking visible-only interval selection at both filter sites
  (`walk_util.select_random_visible` and the `make_walk_html_v2.py` predicate); confirms
  partial, `not_in_frame`, and approximate seed pairs are excluded. The selection behavior
  was already correct; no code change.

- **Corpus integration smoke (manual E2E over `data/outdoor_corpus.txt`):** walks completed
  for IMG_3823, IMG_3830, Conant-4x400, Jason-3200m, Lyra-Hersey-800m; every `walk.html`
  summary rendered numeric metrics or reason tokens with zero bare "?". The 4k/120fps clip
  (Lyra-Wheeling-IMG_3912) is decode-bound; analysis already auto-bins to 960px via
  `common_tools.frame_reader.select_bin_factor_for_analysis`, so the cost is per-frame 4k
  HEVC decode, not analysis overhead. Full suite:
  `pytest tests/ -k "walk or manifest or heat or select_random or layer_order or visible_seed or shebang"`
  -> 283 passed.

## 2026-06-02

### Behavior or Interface Changes

- **`make_walk_html_v2.py` now shows one live rich progress bar per video instead of scrolling per-interval / FWD / BWD log lines, reusing track_runner's solve/refine renderer: `make_walk_html_v2.py`, `walk_driver.py`, `test_blob_walk_v2_no_hermite.py`.** The interval loop in `process_video` is wrapped in `interval_solver.make_solve_progress(interval_solver.FrameETAColumn(frame_counter, total_frames))` (the exact column stack solve/refine use): description + `#`-block bar + M/N + percent + frame-weighted `ETA m:ss  elapsed m:ss`. M/N and percent advance one-per-interval; the ETA is weighted by a shared `frame_counter` list advanced by each interval's inclusive seed-to-seed span (`right - left + 1`), so it stays smooth across intervals ranging from ~10s to several minutes (same split as `solve_queue.py`: count-based M/N, frame-based ETA). One bar per video per invocation (the shell loops videos one at a time); the bar resets per video. Edge cases handled: a resume-skip still advances the bar AND the frame counter (so M/N reaches N and the ETA never looks stuck); an empty visible-both selection skips bar creation entirely (no 0-total bar, no divide-by-zero) and falls through to the no-op writers. To keep the bar uncluttered, the in-loop chatter dropped to DEBUG: the `[idx/N] Interval` line was replaced by the bar, the `Skip (resume)` line is DEBUG, and `walk_driver.run_interval_walk`'s two `Running FWD/BWD walk` lines are DEBUG. Kept at INFO (once per video, before the bar): `Processing video` and the `random-sample: N/M ...` line; the post-loop `Wrote ...` writer lines print after the bar closes. `make_walk_html_v2.py` was added to `_EXCLUDED_FROM_NO_HERMITE_BAN` in `test_blob_walk_v2_no_hermite.py`: it now imports `interval_solver` directly (it already did transitively via `walk_driver`), but only for the progress-UI classes -- no Hermite state, the same driver-tier exception `walk_driver.py` already had. No new dependency (`rich` already in `pip_requirements.txt`); no `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump. Verified: `pyflakes` clean on both modules; live `--walk -v TRACK_VIDEOS/IMG_3823.mkv -n 3 --random-seed 12345` showed a single bar advancing to `3/3 100%` with ETA/elapsed and no FWD/BWD scroll; the same command with `-r` reached `3/3` with `skipped(resume)=3` (bar not stuck); a non-TTY run (`> file 2>&1`) produced a single collapsed bar line with no terminal control-code spam (rich degrades like track_runner); `pytest tests/ -k "manifest or heat or walk or shebang"` 268 passed.

- **`walk_driver.py` demoted to a pure library; its writer blocks extracted to shared functions and wired into `make_walk_html_v2.py` (fixing a silently-broken heat report): `walk_driver.py`, `make_walk_html_v2.py`, `README.md`, [USAGE.md](USAGE.md).** `walk_driver.py` had a standalone `main()`/`parse_args()`/`__main__` CLI that nothing invoked (every live caller used `run_interval_walk` or helpers; `walk_driver.main` appeared only in comments and a README line). That dead CLI was ALSO the only producer of two artifacts the canonical entry point `make_walk_html_v2.py` never wrote: the walker-as-solver `torso_box_coords.npz` and `render_heat_summary.json`. Because `run_random_walk.sh` runs `check_render_manifest.py`, whose heat/seed-cold report reads `render_heat_summary.json`, that report was silently empty on every real run (the file was never written by the script that actually runs). Fix: the three write blocks and the heat-aggregation step were extracted verbatim from `walk_driver.main` into reusable library functions -- `write_solved_intervals_npz(npz_path, accumulated_solved)`, `write_render_manifest(video_output_dir, records)`, `write_heat_summary(video_output_dir, summaries)`, and `summarize_and_strip_heat(left_frame, right_frame, fwd_manifests, bwd_manifests)` (aggregates both directions via `aggregate_interval_heat`, then strips the private `_heat` carrier in place). `main()`, `parse_args()`, and `if __name__ == '__main__'` were removed; the shebang and executable bit were dropped (library module per [PYTHON_STYLE.md](PYTHON_STYLE.md)); now-unused imports (`argparse`, `walk_html`, `walk_io`) were removed; `_video_npz_path` was renamed to public `video_npz_path`. `make_walk_html_v2.process_video` now passes `npz_path` to `run_interval_walk`, accumulates solved entries + heat summaries, and calls the three `write_*` helpers -- so the canonical script writes the npz, render manifest, AND heat summary (its own inline manifest write and the now-unused `import json` were dropped). This also fixes a latent bug where the prior inline manifest write persisted the per-tile `_heat` carrier into `render_manifest.json` (it is now stripped). No `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump (execution-layer + artifact-wiring change; no schema or on-disk format change). Verified: `pyflakes` clean on both modules; `pytest tests/ -k "shebang or no_hermite or bug_101 or walk or manifest or heat or coord_sentinel or select_random"` 279 passed; live `--walk -v TRACK_VIDEOS/IMG_3823.mkv -n 2 --random-seed 12345 -o /tmp/output_smoke_lib` wrote 2 solved intervals to the npz (merged into 713 total), a 40-record `render_manifest.json`, and a 4-record `render_heat_summary.json`, then `check_render_manifest.py -i` printed real per-interval-direction HEAT REPORT / SEED-COLD lines (previously silent) and `PASS`.

- **`check_render_manifest.py` now FAILS loudly when `render_heat_summary.json` is missing beside a `render_manifest.json`, instead of skipping the heat report silently: `check_render_manifest.py`.** The prior code did `if os.path.isfile(heat_summary_file): ... report` -- an absent sibling skipped both the heat and seed-cold reports with no crash, no message, and no exit-code change. That silent skip is precisely what hid the heat report being unwired from the canonical driver for an entire release (see the demotion entry above). Per the repo "fail loudly" rule ([REPO_STYLE.md](REPO_STYLE.md) core philosophies; [PYTHON_STYLE.md](PYTHON_STYLE.md) no-silent-defaults), a manifest without its heat-summary sibling is now appended to the gate failure list with an explicit `render_heat_summary.json sibling missing ... Re-walk.` message and the command exits non-zero. The walk writes the sibling whenever it writes the manifest, so the only ways to hit this are a stale pre-2026-06-02 output dir or a real driver bug -- both of which should be surfaced, not hidden. Verified: deleting the sibling from a passing smoke output and re-running `check_render_manifest.py -i` prints the missing-sibling failure line and exits 1; `pytest tests/ -k "manifest or heat"` 97 passed (the heat tests drive the report/`check_records` functions directly, so they are unaffected).

- **`make_walk_html_v2.py` reduced to a single-video, video-agnostic driver; argparse trimmed from 16 flags to 10: `make_walk_html_v2.py`, `run_random_walk.sh`, `README.md`.** Applies the ARGPARSE-minimalism rule in [PYTHON_STYLE.md](PYTHON_STYLE.md). The driver no longer discovers videos or selects among them; it walks exactly ONE video per invocation (resolved from a path or bare basename via `pathlib.Path(args.video).stem`). Removed flags and their machinery: `--outdoor-400m-only` (+ `filter_outdoor_400m`, the `yaml` import), `--intervals-from-corpus` (+ `load_corpus_intervals`), `--random-sample` / `--no-random-sample` (random visible-both sampling is now the only interval-selection mode; the even-spread default branch and its `walk_util._evenly_spread` call were dropped), `-s` / `--sort-by-size` and `-m` / `--max-videos` / `--limit` (+ `select_videos`, `cost_components`, `_video_path_for`, `print_dry_run`'s multi-video table, the `common_tools.probe_video` import), and the multi-video `-v`/`--videos` `nargs='+'` form (now a single required `-v` / `--video`). `-j` / `--workers` and the video-level `multiprocessing` pool + `_video_worker` were removed because with one video per invocation the pool branch never engaged; videos are walked one-per-invocation by the shell loop, and the walk stays SERIAL over a video's intervals. Interval-level parallelism was intentionally NOT added here: track_runner already owns a worker system (`solver_workers.py` + `solve_queue.py`) and `blob_walk_v2` is being absorbed into track_runner, so a second hand-rolled pool was rejected in favor of converging on that infra later. The discovery constants (`TR_CONFIG_DIR`, `TRACK_VIDEOS_DIR`, `SEEDS_SUFFIX`, `CONFIG_SUFFIX`, `VIDEO_EXTENSIONS`) and the `sys` import are gone. Surviving flags: `-v`/`--video` (required), `-o`/`--output-root`, `-w`/`--walk`, `-n`/`--sample-intervals` (default 20; `<= 0` walks all visible-both), `--random-seed` (kept for reproducible selection), `-r`/`--resume`, `--skip-render`, `-d`/`--dry-run`, `--heat-movie` / `--no-heat-movie`. `process_video` lost its `corpus_left_frames` and `random_sample` params and its corpus/even-spread branches; it always random-samples now. Phase 3 (`walk_html.build_walk_html`) is unchanged and still accumulates across the per-video invocations that share one `--output-root`. No `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump (CLI/driver change only; no on-disk artifact or schema change). The broad per-interval `try/except Exception` block was later removed (see the Fixes entry below). Verified: `pyflakes tools/blob_walk_v2/make_walk_html_v2.py` clean; `--help` shows the 10-flag set; `--dry-run -v TRACK_VIDEOS/Jason-3200m-sectionals-IMG_4005.mkv` resolves the multi-dot basename correctly; live `--walk -v TRACK_VIDEOS/IMG_3823.mkv -n 1 --random-seed 12345 -o /tmp/output_smoke_random20` walked 1/497 visible-both intervals (FWD+BWD), wrote a 28-record `render_manifest.json`, and built `walk.html` (walked=1, errors=0).

- **`run_random_walk.sh` now drives the walk from `data/outdoor_corpus.txt`, one video per call: `run_random_walk.sh`.** The runner reads the corpus file (6 videos), filters comment (`#`) and blank lines with `grep -vE`, strips surrounding whitespace per line, and calls the single-video `make_walk_html_v2.py` once per video with `--walk -v <video> -n 20 -o output_smoke_random20` (6 x 20 = 120 random visible-both intervals total). A `RANDOM_SEED` shell variable (empty by default) is threaded as `--random-seed` only when set, for reproducible selection. The previous version ran `--random-sample -n 20` with NO video list, so it silently fell back to `discover_videos()` and walked every video with a `tr_config` seed file rather than the intended 6 outdoor videos; `outdoor_corpus.txt` was untracked and read by nothing. `CORPUS_FILE` points at `data/outdoor_corpus.txt` (the file was moved into `data/`). The per-video `check_render_manifest.py` heat-report loop over the output subdirectories is unchanged. No code or test change beyond the script.

### Fixes and Maintenance

- **Removed the broad `try/except Exception` from `make_walk_html_v2.process_video`'s interval loop; dropped the now-dead `walk_util._evenly_spread`: `make_walk_html_v2.py`, `walk_util.py`.** The interval loop previously wrapped the whole `run_interval_walk` + accumulate body in `except Exception as e: logger.error(...); counters['errors'] += 1`, swallowing every failure into an error tally -- the exact "fix the symptom" anti-pattern [PYTHON_STYLE.md](PYTHON_STYLE.md) bans (try/except at most ~2 lines; do not catch broad exceptions to continue silently). A failure in `run_interval_walk` is a real bug, not an expected per-interval outcome (an empty `direction_path` is handled inside `run_interval_walk` and never raised), so the catch is gone and exceptions now propagate loudly; the `errors` counter and the `errors=` field in the phase-1+2 summary line were removed. Separately, `walk_util._evenly_spread` was deleted: its only caller was the removed `walk_driver.main` even-spread branch, leaving it with zero references (random visible-both sampling replaced even-spread). Also cleaned a batch of stale comments/docs that an audit surfaced after the `walk_driver` demotion: the `walk_driver.py` import-bootstrap comment no longer claims "run directly", the "Both entry points (walk_driver.main and ...)" and three "mirrors walk_driver.main" comments were corrected, `README.md` now states a missing `render_heat_summary.json` sibling is a hard failure (was "skips cleanly"), [COORDINATE_SPACES.md](COORDINATE_SPACES.md) marks `walk_driver.main` as removed, and `check_render_manifest.py`'s FAIL summary noun changed from "failing tile(s)" to "failing check(s)" (a missing sibling is not a tile). `run_random_walk.sh` uses `set -eo pipefail` (NOT `-u`): `set -u` aborted on the first video because `source source_me.sh` pulls in `bash_completion.sh`, which references the interactive-only `$PS1` (unset here). Verified: `pyflakes` clean on all touched modules; live `--walk -v TRACK_VIDEOS/IMG_3823.mkv -n 1 -o /tmp/output_smoke_noexc` ran `walked=1, skipped(resume)=0` and built `walk.html`; `pytest tests/ -k "manifest or heat or no_hermite or walk or markdown or select_random or shebang"` 275 passed.

## 2026-05-30

### Additions and New Features

- **Two derived stats added to per-interval heat summary: `heat_present_pct` and `mean_heat`: `walk_driver.py`, `check_render_manifest.py`, `README.md`, `test_check_render_manifest_seed_cold.py`, `test_check_render_manifest_heat.py`.** Extends the per-interval `render_heat_summary.json` with two new fields. `heat_present_pct` (float in [0,1]): fraction of eligible frames that are heat-present; 0.0 when `heat_eligible == 0`. `mean_heat` (float or null): mean of per-frame hot-pixel means across heat-present frames; null when no heat-present frames. Both are C13-approved interval-level floats stored in JSON (not per-frame, not in the npz). The per-frame `in_box_hot_mean` float is re-threaded in-memory into the `_heat` carrier in `walk_driver._render_direction_tiles` (`hot_mean` key alongside the existing `computed`/`hot_count`) so `aggregate_interval_heat` can compute the mean; the carrier is deleted before `render_manifest.json` is written, so per-frame float heat is still NOT persisted per-tile (C13 unchanged). `aggregate_interval_heat` now collects the heat-present frame list, computes the fraction, and averages `hot_mean` over those frames. `report_heat_fraction` in `check_render_manifest.py` updated to read both new keys (direct key access, no `.get` defaults) and include them in the HEAT REPORT line: `heat-present N/M (X%); mean_heat=<float or n/a>`. Two new focused tests added to `test_check_render_manifest_seed_cold.py`: `test_aggregate_heat_present_pct_and_mean_heat` (3 present frames with hot_means 10/20/30 + 1 cold + 1 not-computed -> mean_heat==20.0, heat_present_pct==0.75); `test_aggregate_zero_present_frames` (2 computed-cold -> mean_heat is None, heat_present_pct==0.0). Existing tests updated: `_summary` helper in `test_check_render_manifest_heat.py` extended to include the two new fields (defaults auto-computed); `_make_tile` in `test_check_render_manifest_seed_cold.py` gains `hot_mean=None` kwarg; test output assertions updated for the new HEAT REPORT format. No npz change. No `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump. No walk run. No commit. Verified: `pytest tests/ -k "heat or seed_cold or manifest or in_box" -q` 103 passed, 1364 deselected in 3.80s; `pyflakes tools/blob_walk_v2/core/walk_driver.py tools/blob_walk_v2/check_render_manifest.py tests/test_check_render_manifest_seed_cold.py tests/test_check_render_manifest_heat.py` clean (no output).

- **Seed-cold report added to manifest gate (report-only): `check_render_manifest.py`, `test_check_render_manifest_seed_cold.py`.** New pure helper `count_seed_cold(records) -> (seed_cold_frames, seed_eligible_count)` identifies human seed tiles where `in_box_heat_computed is True` AND `in_box_heat_present is False` -- a strong "box parked on background / stale annotation" signal. Seed tiles where heat was not computed are excluded (unknown, not cold). New printer `report_seed_cold(records, source, direction)` mirrors `report_heat_fraction`'s older-manifest guard (silently skips when no record carries `in_box_heat_computed`), calls the helper, and prints one `SEED-COLD {source} [{direction}]: N/M eligible seed tiles are heat-cold; frames=[...]` line per direction (prints `no eligible seed tiles` when seed_eligible_count==0 so the check is always visible). Called in `check_records` right after `report_heat_fraction` in the per-direction loop. No exit-code change. No gate modification. No `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump (reuses existing manifest fields; no on-disk artifact change). Focused pytest covers all four cases: cold seed in list; warm seed eligible but not cold; not-computed seed excluded from both counts; non-seed cold tile excluded. Verified: `pytest tests/ -k "seed_cold" -q` 5 passed, 1462 deselected; `pyflakes tools/blob_walk_v2/check_render_manifest.py tests/test_check_render_manifest_seed_cold.py` clean (no output). No walk run. No commit. SUPERSEDED later this same day by the C13 heat-summary rework (see the Behavior or Interface Changes entry below): the per-tile `count_seed_cold` helper and per-tile heat reads were removed; seed-cold is now aggregated into `render_heat_summary.json` and reported per interval-direction.

- **Random visible-both interval sampling added: `walk_util.py`, `make_walk_html_v2.py`, `run_random_walk.sh`.** New `walk_util.select_random_visible(post_start, n, rng)` pure helper picks up to `n` random intervals where both bracketing seeds have `status == "visible"` (strict: "partial" and "not_in_frame" are excluded). If visible count <= n all visible intervals are returned; otherwise `rng.sample(visible, n)` is used, then sorted by left_seed `frame_index` for stable output ordering. `make_walk_html_v2.py` gains `--random-sample` / `--no-random-sample` boolean pair (default off) and `--random-seed` (type int, default None) for reproducible selection; `import random` added. When `--random-sample` is active and no corpus filter is set, a `random.Random(random_seed)` instance is built (None gives a fresh nondeterministic run) and `walk_util.select_random_visible` is called with the `-n` / `--sample-intervals-per-video` count; the corpus-filter and default `_evenly_spread` branches are unchanged. The new branch logs `random-sample: N/M visible-both intervals selected (n=N, rng_seed=S)` mirroring the corpus-filter log format. `run_random_walk.sh` replaces the corpus-pinned `run_120corpus_walk.sh` (renamed via `git mv`): uses `--random-sample -n 20`, writes to `output_smoke_random20`, includes a comment showing how to pin the RNG with `--random-seed 12345`, retains the per-video `check_render_manifest.py` heat-report loop. New focused pytest `test_walk_util_select_random_visible.py` covers: visible-both filter excludes partial-ended and not_in_frame-ended intervals; two calls with the same `random.Random(42)` return the same chosen `left_frame` set; n >= visible count returns all visible. Verified: `pytest tests/ -k "random_visible or visible_interval or select_random" -q` 3 passed; `pyflakes tools/blob_walk_v2/walk_util.py tools/blob_walk_v2/make_walk_html_v2.py tests/test_walk_util_select_random_visible.py` clean (no output); `python3 tools/blob_walk_v2/make_walk_html_v2.py --help` shows `--random-sample`, `--no-random-sample`, and `--random-seed`. Walk not run (another walk in progress on this machine).

### Behavior or Interface Changes

- **In-box heat moved off per-frame JSON to a per-interval `render_heat_summary.json` (contract C13): `walk_driver.py`, `check_render_manifest.py`, `README.md`.** Reverses the persistence half of the M1-B/M1-C/M1-G manifest-heat work landed earlier this session. Contract C13 routes dense frame data to the `.npz` (integers) and interval data to JSON; in-box heat is frame-derived but SPARSE (only walk-observed frames have a value -- interpolated, extrapolated, soft-miss, and render-only frames do not), so it fits neither a dense npz array nor per-frame JSON. The five per-tile heat fields (`in_box_hot_mean`, `in_box_hot_count`, `in_box_heat_present`, `in_box_heat_computed`, `heat_threshold_used`) are no longer written to `render_manifest.json`. Instead `walk_driver` aggregates heat to ONE summary per `(interval, direction)` and writes a sibling `render_heat_summary.json` (interval data, JSON-approved). New pure helper `walk_driver.aggregate_interval_heat(manifests, left_frame, right_frame, direction, threshold)` reduces a private in-memory `_heat` carrier (`{computed, hot_count}`, stashed on each per-tile dict by `_render_direction_tiles`) into `{left_frame, right_frame, direction, heat_eligible, heat_present, not_computed, seed_cold_frames, threshold}`. `heat_eligible` is the denominator (frames the walk observed); `not_computed = total - eligible` surfaces sparsity as coverage rather than hiding it; `seed_cold_frames` lists seed tiles that were computed but cold (`hot_count == 0`). AGGREGATION SEAM: done in the per-video interval loop (not in `run_interval_walk`) because that loop is the single place that already knows `left_frame`/`right_frame` AND holds both `fwd_manifests`/`bwd_manifests`; the loop calls the aggregator per direction then deletes the `_heat` carrier so `render_manifest.json` carries NO heat keys. The float `in_box_hot_mean` is DROPPED from persistence entirely -- it was the C13 per-frame-float violation and the reports never used it; the walk-time compute in `walk_walker` is unchanged (still cached in-memory, just no longer threaded to the renderer). `check_render_manifest.py`: the two hard gates (conversion_count==1, non-seed-missing-solved-box) still read `render_manifest.json` per-tile records, exit code unchanged; `report_heat_fraction`/`report_seed_cold` rewritten to read the sibling `render_heat_summary.json` and print one `HEAT REPORT ... [L,R] [dir]: heat-present N/M eligible (P%); K not-computed; threshold=...` and one `SEED-COLD ... [L,R] [dir]: C seed tiles heat-cold; frames=[...]` line per interval-direction (report-only, no exit-code change). A run with no `render_heat_summary.json` sibling (older run) skips both reports cleanly. The per-tile pure helper `count_seed_cold` is replaced by `report_heat_summaries` + a small pure `heat_summary_present` helper; the README per-tile 5-field table is replaced by the interval-summary doc. No `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump and no npz change: the npz schema and `state_io` are untouched (heat stays out of the npz, integers only), and `render_manifest.json`/`render_heat_summary.json` are regenerated render artifacts, not schema'd solved data. Verified: `pytest tests/ -k "heat or seed_cold or manifest or in_box" -q` 101 passed, 1364 deselected in 3.60s; `pyflakes tools/blob_walk_v2/core/walk_driver.py tools/blob_walk_v2/check_render_manifest.py track_runner/blob_walk/walk_walker.py tests/test_check_render_manifest_seed_cold.py tests/test_check_render_manifest_heat.py tests/test_m1d_heat_not_computed_detection.py` clean (no output); `grep` confirms no stale per-tile heat readers/tests/docs remain outside the walk_walker compute primitive and comments. No walk run. No commit.

### Fixes and Maintenance

- **M2-B style fix: replaced broad `try/except Exception` + manual scratch-dir helpers with `tempfile.TemporaryDirectory` context manager in `encode_heat_movie_for_direction`: `heat_movie_encode.py`.** The prior implementation used a 40-line `try: ... except Exception: _delete_scratch_dir(...); raise` block to clean up the scratch dir on failure, plus a trailing `_delete_scratch_dir(...)` on the success path. This exceeded the repo's "try/except at most ~2 lines" rule. Replaced with a `tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(prefix=f"heat_movie_{direction_label_lower}_", dir=scratch_parent)` context manager that owns the scratch dir for the whole spill->encode->copy->verify sequence (Steps 3-7); cleanup fires automatically on both normal exit and any exception. The injectable `scratch_parent` parameter is preserved (passed as `dir=scratch_parent`). The per-direction `prefix=` kwarg preserves the diagnostic label in the dir name. `dest_path` is outside the scratch dir and survives context exit. Both `_make_scratch_dir` and `_delete_scratch_dir` are now dead code with no callers and were deleted (only user was this file). `import tempfile` was already present. Verified: `pyflakes tools/blob_walk_v2/render/heat_movie_encode.py` clean (no output); `grep -n "except Exception"` exits 1 (no match); `pytest tests/ -k "heat or walk or manifest or coord"` 300 passed (excluding pre-existing pyflakes warn in e2e_heat_movie_smoke.py line 300 f-string-no-placeholder which pre-dates this change); `python3 tests/e2e/e2e_heat_movie_smoke.py` PASS (dest non-empty, scratch removed, no leak).

- **Audit follow-up to the `tempfile.TemporaryDirectory` refactor: a defanged leak assertion and stale docstrings corrected in the former `e2e_heat_movie_smoke.py` and `heat_movie_encode.py`.** A 6-pass code audit found the refactor changed the scratch-dir name from `<scratch_parent>/fwd` to `<scratch_parent>/heat_movie_fwd_<random>` (the `TemporaryDirectory(prefix=...)` scheme), which silently turned the e2e smoke's post-run leak assertion into a vacuous always-true check (`os.path.exists(<parent>/fwd)` for a path that is now never created). LEARNING POINT: an internal rename can defang a test assertion that hardcodes the old name without the test ever failing; the leak check now derives the path pattern instead of hardcoding it. Fixes: in `e2e_heat_movie_smoke.py`, the vacuous leak assertion was replaced with a real `glob.glob('<SCRATCH_PARENT>/heat_movie_fwd_*')`-empty check, the now-dead `_collect_bgr_paths` helper was deleted, and stale comments referencing the deleted `_make_scratch_dir` and the old `<parent>/fwd` layout were removed. In `heat_movie_encode.py`, the module and function docstrings were corrected (cleanup is now unconditional whole-dir deletion on both normal exit and exception via the context manager, not the old selective `.bgr`-frame delete conditional on verifications passing), and the stdlib imports were reordered so `tempfile` precedes `subprocess` (length-then-alpha). No `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump (test and docstring/import-order changes only; no on-disk artifact or schema change). Verified: `pyflakes` clean on both files; `pytest tests/ -k "heat or walk or manifest or coord" -q` 343 passed, 1129 deselected; `python3 tests/e2e/e2e_heat_movie_smoke.py` PASS (`PASS: heat-movie smoke test passed.`) with the new no-leak line.

- **M1-G: in-box heat now CACHED at walk time instead of re-derived at render time (supersedes M1-F): [walk_walker.py](../track_runner/blob_walk/walk_walker.py), `walk_driver.py`.** Deriving the residual has a high computational cost, so it is better to cache the scalars rather than recompute. M1-F fixed the inert-metric blocker by re-deriving the residual at render time via `residual_heat_map.compute_heat_map_roi`, but that paid an extra per-frame decode the walk had already paid during solving. M1-G removes that recompute. The residual is still LIVE on the `BlobObserverTrace` (`residual_dog`, `validity_mask`, `roi_origin_xy`) during the walk, before `walk_walker.lighten_trace` drops it; M1-G measures heat there, against the FINAL Viterbi-solved box, and caches the scalars on the per-frame `direction_path` entry for the renderer to read. New helper `walk_walker.measure_in_box_heat_for_frame(live_trace, cx, cy, w, h) -> (hot_mean, hot_count)` calls the frozen M1-A primitive `common_tools.in_box_heat.measure_in_box_heat` with the live `residual_dog`/`validity_mask`/`roi_origin_xy` and the FINAL solved box (a typed `common_tools.coord_space.ProcessedBox`); returns `(None, 0)` when no live trace or `residual_dog is None`. The single-source threshold is `residual_motion.DEFAULT_THRESHOLD`. Two emit sites call it: the windowed emit loop (against the emitted `r["cx"], r["cy"], frame_w, frame_h`) and the bootstrap step (against the seed box, measured before `lighten_trace`); both attach `in_box_hot_mean` and `in_box_hot_count` to the `direction_path` entry dict. `_build_window_entry` now also carries the live trace as `entry["live_trace"]` IN ADDITION to the lightened copy, so the emit loop can read the residual; the stored `direction_trace_map` still gets the lightened copy, so the memory bound is unchanged (only the <=9 in-buffer window frames hold a live residual at once). In `walk_driver._render_direction_tiles`, the M1-F `compute_heat_map_roi` re-derive block is REMOVED; the `--walk` branch threads the cached `in_box_hot_mean`/`in_box_hot_count` through `direction_box_by_frame`, and the heat block reads them: `in_box_heat_computed=True` when a solved box AND cached scalars are present (a real walk), else False (render-only npz has no cached scalars). All five manifest fields are unchanged (`in_box_hot_mean`, `in_box_hot_count`, `in_box_heat_present` = count>0, `in_box_heat_computed`, `heat_threshold_used`). The now-unused `import common_tools.in_box_heat` and `import residual_heat_map` were removed from `walk_driver.py` (the M2-A heat MOVIE keeps its own `compute_heat_map_roi` path, out of scope). Not touched: the M1-A primitive, `residual_motion`, `residual_heat_map`, `check_render_manifest`, `lighten_trace`'s lightening behavior. No `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump (additive render-diagnostic fields, names and meanings unchanged). Verified: `pyflakes track_runner/blob_walk/walk_walker.py tools/blob_walk_v2/core/walk_driver.py` clean; `pytest tests/ -k "walk or heat or manifest"` 235 passed, 1196 deselected, 1 warning in 2.85s; `compute_heat_map_roi` no longer referenced anywhere in walk_driver.py or walk_walker.py (grep exit 1); ACCEPTANCE PROOF -- live `--walk` on IMG_3823 interval [1535, 1547] via `make_walk_html_v2.py -w -v IMG_3823 -n 1 -o output_smoke`, then `check_render_manifest.py -i output_smoke/IMG_3823` reports `HEAT REPORT ... [FWD]: heat-present 12/12 eligible (100.0%); 1 not-computed (skipped); total 13 tiles; threshold=10.0` and the same for BWD, identical eligibility to M1-F but now with no render-time decode.

- **M1-F: in-box heat metric was inert on every live `--walk`; now re-derived at render time: `walk_driver.py`.** Fixes the M1-D blocker. The M1-B metric read `trace.residual_dog`, but every trace in `direction_trace_map` is passed through `walk_walker.lighten_trace`, which sets `residual_dog=None`, `residual_pre_dog=None`, `validity_mask=None` to bound memory (intended WS2-A behavior). The eligibility test `has_live_residual = (trace.residual_dog is not None)` was therefore always False, so `in_box_heat_computed` was False on every frame (M1-D measured 568 tiles, 0 computed=true across 4 videos). The fix, in `_render_direction_tiles`, re-derives the residual at render time against the SOLVED box, mirroring exactly what M2-A's `render/heat_movie_frame.py` does: it calls `residual_heat_map.compute_heat_map_roi(reader, frame_index, scene_transform, pred_center=(solved cx, cy), pred_box=(solved w, h), fps, threshold=residual_motion.DEFAULT_THRESHOLD, out_arrays=d)`, and on a non-None return reads `d["residual_dog"]`, `d["validity_mask"]`, and the ROI origin from `d["roi_bounds"]` (the (x1, y1) corner), then calls the frozen M1-A primitive `common_tools.in_box_heat.measure_in_box_heat(residual_dog, validity_mask, roi_origin, box=ProcessedBox(solved cx,cy,w,h), threshold=DEFAULT_THRESHOLD)`. New eligibility: `in_box_heat_computed=True` when a solved box is present AND the residual was successfully derived (compute_heat_map_roi returned non-None with reader/scene_transform/fps available). NOT-COMPUTED (`computed=False`) when there is no solved box, no reader/scene_transform (render-only npz mode), or compute_heat_map_roi returned None (interval-edge frame); these keep `hot_mean=None, hot_count=0, present=False`. The single-source threshold (`residual_motion.DEFAULT_THRESHOLD`) is used for both the primitive call and `heat_threshold_used`; all five manifest field names are unchanged. `lighten_trace` and trace lightening are NOT touched (the memory bound stays); the M1-A primitive, residual_motion, residual_heat_map, and check_render_manifest are NOT modified. `reader`, `scene_transform`, and `fps` were already parameters of `_render_direction_tiles`, so no threading was needed. SUPERSESSION: this adds a bounded extra decode per rendered frame via `compute_heat_map_roi`, superseding the plan's earlier "no extra decode" assumption (which was premised on `residual_dog` being in scope before lightening removed it); the decode shares residual_motion's bounded gray cache (`MAX_GRAY_CACHE_FRAMES`), so it stays bounded. The stale comment at the old lines 626-632 (claiming "A live --walk trace carries residual_dog") was corrected. No `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump (additive render-diagnostic computation only; field names and meanings unchanged). Verified: `pyflakes tools/blob_walk_v2/core/walk_driver.py` clean; `pytest tests/ -k "walk or heat or manifest"` 235 passed, 1196 deselected, 1 warning in 2.84s; ACCEPTANCE PROOF -- live `--walk` on IMG_3823 interval [1535, 1547] via `make_walk_html_v2.py -w -v IMG_3823 -n 1 -o output_smoke`, then `check_render_manifest.py -i output_smoke/IMG_3823` reports `HEAT REPORT ... [FWD]: heat-present 12/12 eligible (100.0%); 1 not-computed (skipped); total 13 tiles; threshold=10.0` and the same for BWD (before the fix: 0 eligible tiles corpus-wide). The 1 not-computed per direction is the interval-edge frame where compute_heat_map_roi returns None.

### Developer Tests and Notes

- **M1-D corpus heat-present fraction measurement (Patch 4): `test_m1d_heat_not_computed_detection.py`.** MEASUREMENT AND REPORT RUN. Corpus: 24corpus_v2 (6 videos; Conant-4x400-2026_April_15, IMG_3823, IMG_3830, Jason-3200m-sectionals-IMG_4005, Lyra-Hersey-800m-IMG_3882, Lyra-Wheeling-IMG_3912), 4 intervals per video from `dump_step1/24corpus/`, run via `python3 tools/blob_walk_v2/make_walk_html_v2.py --walk -o output_smoke --intervals-from-corpus dump_step1/24corpus/ -j 2`. 4 of 6 videos completed by report time; 2 still running. VERBATIM HEAT REPORT (4 videos, 568 tiles): ALL tiles NOT-COMPUTED -- `heat-present 0/0 eligible (n/a (no eligible tiles)); N not-computed (skipped); threshold=10.0` for every (source, direction) pair. STRUCTURAL FINDING: `in_box_heat_computed=False` for 100% of tiles across all 4 completed videos in BOTH directions. Root cause: `walk_walker.lighten_trace` drops `residual_dog=None` from every trace BEFORE it is stored in `direction_trace_map`; the driver's heat computation (`has_live_residual = trace.residual_dog is not None`) then always evaluates False, so `in_box_heat_computed=False` and the tile is NOT-COMPUTED for every frame. `trace_present` (the corridor_blobs field) IS populated (Conant FWD: 60/98 tiles with blobs; BWD: 64/98), confirming the walker ran and observed blobs, but `residual_dog` was dropped before the manifest phase. SEED-RESIDUAL VERIFICATION CONCLUSION: `trace.residual_dog` on seed frames is computed on the SAME temporal basis as non-seed frames. The bootstrap step (`_run_bootstrap_step`) calls `_compute_roi_and_observe` (which calls `observe_blob_at`) at the seed frame itself (step=0), then `lighten_trace` drops `residual_dog` from the result before storing. The neighbor seed frame is NOT observed by the walker (the loop breaks when `frame_f == neighbor_seed_frame`). Therefore: seed frames and non-seed frames both have their residual_dog dropped by lighten_trace; the "seed residual" question is moot because NO frame's residual_dog survives. The neighbor-seed frame has no trace at all (it is the other interval's anchor). SEED VS NON-SEED SPLIT: both equally NOT-COMPUTED (seeds: 8 per direction per video; non-seeds: varied by interval length). SKIPPED COUNT: 100% of tiles skipped per direction. THRESHOLD: 10.0 (DEFAULT_THRESHOLD) in all manifests. SUSPICIOUS INTERVALS: all intervals are equally suspicious -- the issue is structural, not per-interval. DESIGN DEFECT: the heat measurement in M1-B must be computed DURING the walk (before lighten_trace runs) rather than during the render phase (which reads the lightened trace). The correct fix (deferred to a follow-up coder task) is to compute `(hot_mean, hot_count)` inside `_compute_roi_and_observe` or immediately after `observe_blob_at` returns in the bootstrap step and walk loop, store it in the lightened trace or a parallel per-frame dict, and then read it in the manifest phase. Alternatively, compute heat from `residual_heat_map.compute_heat_map_roi` during the render phase (re-derives residual from the reader), which is the approach that does NOT depend on the stored trace. FUTURE FLOOR CANDIDATES: cannot be set until M1-B defect is fixed; a re-run with corrected heat computation is required before any floor is meaningful. TEST FILE ADDED: 5 passing structural tests confirm (1) lighten_trace drops residual_dog; (2) lighten_trace drops validity_mask; (3) lighten_trace preserves blobs/origin; (4) has_live_residual=False for lightened trace; (5) NOT-COMPUTED tiles are correctly excluded from denominator by check_render_manifest with PASS exit code. `pytest tests/test_m1d_heat_not_computed_detection.py` 5 passed in 0.23s. FULL VERBATIM OUTPUT from 4-video run: `PASS: 568 tiles in 4 manifest(s); every non-seed tile has a solved box and conversion_count == 1` (gate passes; heat report shows all not-computed). Lyra-Hersey and Lyra-Wheeling outputs will be identical by the same code path.

### Additions and New Features

- **120-corpus shell runner added: `run_120corpus_walk.sh`.** Single-purpose bash script that walks the full 120-interval corpus (`dump_step1/120corpus/`) via `make_walk_html_v2.py --walk -o output_smoke_120corpus --intervals-from-corpus dump_step1/120corpus/ -j 4`, then loops over each per-video subdirectory and runs `check_render_manifest.py -i` to print the in-box motion-heat fraction report. Uses `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` for the repo root; sources `source_me.sh` before each Python call; no flags (single-purpose runner, constants hardcoded). Run directly: `./run_120corpus_walk.sh`.

- **E2E smoke for --heat-movie pipeline added (M2-C, Patch 8): former `e2e_heat_movie_smoke.py`.** Non-browser E2E that exercises the full spill->ffmpeg->copy->cleanup pipeline end-to-end via `encode_heat_movie_for_direction` with a synthetic reader and a 5-frame direction_path (no real corpus video required). `residual_heat_map.compute_heat_map_roi` is replaced at module level before the call so the test runs offline. Assertions: (1) returned `dest_path` is a non-empty string (movie path reported); (2) destination `heat_fwd.mkv` exists and is non-empty; (3) every `.bgr` file is exactly `roiW*roiH*3` bytes -- verified by spilling a center box and an off-frame edge box into a controlled temp dir and statting each file, confirming constant ROI shape independent of box position; (4) no run-scoped scratch directory remains after the run (post-run cleanup check on `<SCRATCH_PARENT>/fwd`). Skips cleanly (exit 0 + message) when ffmpeg is absent via `shutil.which`. Scratch output goes under `/tmp/output_smoke_heat_movie` (stable name, reused across runs). Run directly: `source source_me.sh && python3 tests/e2e/e2e_heat_movie_smoke.py`. Not collected by pytest (`conftest.py` `collect_ignore = ["e2e", "playwright"]`; naming-conventions test passes). Verified: direct run PASS (roi_size=(480,480), expected_bgr_bytes=691200, dest_size=3121 bytes non-empty, .bgr path count=5, byte size OK for center+edge, scratch dir removed); `pytest tests/ -k heat -q` 43 passed, 1383 deselected; naming-conventions test 5 passed. ffmpeg present at `/opt/homebrew/bin/ffmpeg`; encode ran (not skipped).

- **Docs: `--heat-movie` flag documented (M2-D, Patch 9): [USAGE.md](USAGE.md) + `README.md`.** Added "Heat movie diagnostic (blob_walk_v2)" section to `docs/USAGE.md`: how to invoke `--heat-movie`, what it produces (`heat_fwd.mkv` and `heat_bwd.mkv` beside each interval's render tiles), that ffmpeg is required only for `--heat-movie` (not for normal `--walk`), memory model (one frame at a time, ROI-only, within-run `/tmp` scratch deleted after each interval encode), and `brew install ffmpeg` install hint. Added `--heat-movie` / `--no-heat-movie` row to the key-flags table in `tools/blob_walk_v2/README.md` plus a "Heat movie output" subsection describing the per-direction `.mkv` outputs and the ffmpeg requirement. No code or test changes.

- **`--heat-movie` flag + ffmpeg encode + cleanup added (M2-B, Patch 7): `heat_movie_encode.py`, `walk_driver.py`, `make_walk_html_v2.py`.** Wires the M2-A fixed-ROI compositor into a usable CLI diagnostic. New `heat_movie_encode.py` module (pure encode helper, never imported by the normal walk path): `check_ffmpeg_available()` returns the absolute ffmpeg path or raises a clean `RuntimeError` when ffmpeg is absent; `encode_heat_movie_for_direction(direction_label, left_seed_box, right_seed_box, direction_path, reader, scene_transform, fps, output_interval_dir, scratch_parent=None)` orchestrates the full pipeline -- (1) compute fixed `(roiW, roiH)` once from the two bracketing seeds via `heat_movie_frame.compute_fixed_heat_roi_size`; (2) create a run-scoped scratch dir under `/tmp` (injectable `scratch_parent` for tests); (3) spill per-frame `.bgr` files via `build_heat_movie_frame` for each frame in `direction_path`; (4) run ffmpeg `image2` encode with `-f image2 -vcodec rawvideo -framerate <fps> -s <roiW>x<roiH> -pix_fmt bgr24 -i frame_%06d.bgr -c:v libx264 -pix_fmt yuv420p heat.mkv` via `subprocess.run` + returncode check (`-vcodec rawvideo` is required because the `.bgr` extension is not auto-decoded by `image2`); (5) verify `/tmp/heat.mkv` is non-empty; (6) copy the finished `.mkv` beside the per-interval render output as `heat_<direction>.mkv`; (7) verify the destination is non-empty; (8) delete the scratch dir ONLY after both verifications pass. On any failure: scratch dir is deleted and the exception is re-raised. `walk_driver.py` changes: `parse_args()` adds `--heat-movie` / `--no-heat-movie` (default off via `set_defaults`); a lazily-imported `heat_movie_encode.check_ffmpeg_available()` is called in `parse_args()` when `--heat-movie` is set so ffmpeg-missing is diagnosed at startup; `run_interval_walk` gains `heat_movie: bool = False` and `heat_movie_scratch_parent: str | None = None` parameters; after `fwd_raw`/`bwd_raw` are assigned (post-walk, post-tile-render) the heat-movie encode block fires if `heat_movie=True` and the respective direction_path is non-empty (FWD and BWD independent, C9); each encode is wrapped in a per-direction try/except that logs the error and continues rather than aborting the whole interval; `main()` threads `args.heat_movie` through to `run_interval_walk`. `make_walk_html_v2.py` changes: `parse_args()` adds the same `--heat-movie` / `--no-heat-movie` pair; `main()` calls `check_ffmpeg_available()` lazily when `--heat-movie` is set; `process_video` gains `heat_movie: bool = False` and threads it into `run_interval_walk`; `_video_worker` and the payloads tuple are updated to include `heat_movie`. Normal `--walk` path unchanged: `heat_movie_encode` is never imported when `--heat-movie` is absent, confirmed by the lazy import pattern. Extra frame reads: `build_heat_movie_frame` calls `compute_heat_map_roi` which decodes through the bounded gray cache (`residual_motion.MAX_GRAY_CACHE_FRAMES`); no new frame-limit constant. Verified: `pyflakes tools/blob_walk_v2/render/heat_movie_encode.py tools/blob_walk_v2/core/walk_driver.py tools/blob_walk_v2/make_walk_html_v2.py` clean; `pytest tests/ -k "walk or heat"` 230 passed, 1196 deselected in 3.14s; `/tmp`-only encode smoke test PASS (3 synthetic 40x40 bgr24 frames -> 1413-byte non-empty heat.mkv, scratch dir deleted). ffmpeg present on this machine at `/opt/homebrew/bin/ffmpeg` (v8.1.1). No `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump (no on-disk artifact or schema change; `.mkv` is a new optional diagnostic output, not a schema-versioned artifact).

- **Fixed-ROI heat-movie frame compositor + raw-BGR spill added (M2-A, Patch 6): `heat_movie_frame.py` + `test_blob_walk_v2_heat_movie_frame.py`.** Builds the per-frame imagery for the OPTIONAL `--heat-movie` diagnostic (the `--heat-movie` CLI flag, driver wiring, and the ffmpeg encode are the SEPARATE later task M2-B; this patch adds NO flag, NO subprocess, and NO ffmpeg call). Two public functions M2-B will call: (1) `compute_fixed_heat_roi_size(seed1_box, seed2_box) -> (roiW, roiH)` derives ONE constant square window for the whole interval from the LARGER of the two bracketing seeds by torso height, reusing the solver's ROI rule (`residual_motion.ROI_MULTIPLIER` and the same min-half-side floor of 50 px as `residual_motion._compute_roi`); it intentionally omits `_compute_roi`'s frame clamp so the size stays constant across the interval (edge clamping is per-frame black-fill, not size change), giving ffmpeg one `-s WxH`. (2) `build_heat_movie_frame(reader, frame_index, scene_transform, solved_box, roi_size, scratch_dir, fps, threshold=residual_motion.DEFAULT_THRESHOLD) -> path` builds a BGR image whose shape is ALWAYS exactly `(roiH, roiW, 3)`: a fixed `(roiW, roiH)` window centered on the solved `ProcessedBox`, with the JET motion-cue heat from `residual_heat_map.compute_heat_map_roi` pasted in by ABSOLUTE PROCESSED pixel coordinates (composite origin minus window origin, no shift, no scale), the solved torso box drawn with `walk_draw` styling (the `walk_render` "blended" prediction color, dashed + normal, via the single `processed_box_to_tile_local` conversion using the window origin as the ROI origin), and the M1-A in-box hot-mean drawn as text. The hot-mean comes from the FROZEN M1-A primitive `common_tools.in_box_heat.measure_in_box_heat`, fed the `residual_dog`/`validity_mask`/`roi_bounds` that `compute_heat_map_roi` returns via its `out_arrays` side-channel, with the SAME single-source threshold `residual_motion.DEFAULT_THRESHOLD` the manifest metric uses (imported, never hardcoded). Where the fixed window extends past a frame edge, or where the heat composite does not cover, pixels stay BLACK-FILLED; the window is NEVER shifted (that moves the box off-center) and NEVER scaled (that distorts overlay coordinates). When `compute_heat_map_roi` returns None (residual-unavailable edge frame), the output is still the fixed-shape window (black base + solved box + an "n/a" hot label), byte-identical in size to a normal frame. The finished frame is written to `<scratch_dir>/frame_%06d.bgr` as contiguous raw headerless BGR bytes (exactly `roiW*roiH*3`, zero-padded index for ffmpeg's `image2`) and the path is returned; the array is not retained. Memory: one frame is built, written, and dropped -- nothing accumulates; decode stays capped by the existing bounded gray cache (`residual_motion.MAX_GRAY_CACHE_FRAMES`) inside `compute_heat_map_roi`, so NO new frame-limit constant is introduced. Coordinate note: everything is PROCESSED-space; this OPTIONAL compositor MAY read frame imagery, but only through `compute_heat_map_roi` (the center frame plus its warp neighbors via the bounded gray cache) -- the M1 metric path decodes no extra frames and is untouched. New test file (4 tests, fully offline by monkeypatching `compute_heat_map_roi`): window size is order-independent and follows the taller seed; every written file is exactly `roiW*roiH*3` bytes for a centered box AND a near-edge box where black-fill triggers; reloaded raw bytes reshape to a constant `(roiH, roiW, 3)` across several box positions including frame corners; a `compute_heat_map_roi`-None edge frame still yields the fixed-shape byte count. Verified: `pytest tests/ -k heat` 43 passed, 1383 deselected in 2.61s (the 4 new tests included); `pyflakes tools/blob_walk_v2/render/heat_movie_frame.py tests/test_blob_walk_v2_heat_movie_frame.py` clean. No `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump (new diagnostic compositor; no on-disk artifact or schema change -- the `.bgr` spill is a within-run scratch cache per C13, owned and deleted by M2-B).

- **Per-interval heat-present fraction REPORT added to manifest gate (M1-C, Patch 3): `check_render_manifest.py` + `test_check_render_manifest_heat.py`.** `check_records` now aggregates per `(source, direction)` the fraction of ELIGIBLE tiles that are heat-present, where eligible means `in_box_heat_computed == true`. Computed-cold tiles (`computed=true, present=false`) stay in the denominator; only not-computed tiles (`computed=false`, e.g. render-only npz or no solved box) are excluded. Prints one `HEAT REPORT <manifest> [<direction>]: heat-present N/M eligible (X%); K not-computed (skipped); total T tiles; threshold=<value>` line per direction per manifest. This is report-only: the heat fraction does NOT set the exit code; the existing two gates (conversion_count==1 and non-seed-missing-solved-box) alone govern PASS/FAIL and process exit status. Older manifests without `in_box_heat_computed` on any record are silently skipped (no crash). Implementation uses explicit key-presence checks, not silent `dict.get` defaults, so a real unexpected missing field surfaces immediately. Two new helpers: `_group_by_direction(records)` groups by direction field (falls back to "unknown" for pre-direction manifests); `report_heat_fraction(records, source, direction)` computes and prints the report. New fixture test (5 tests): warm interval (3/3 eligible, 100%); computed-cold interval (0/2 eligible, 0%, exit PASS); not-computed interval (0 eligible, n/a, 2 skipped, exit PASS); older manifest without heat fields (no HEAT REPORT line, no crash); existing gate failure (conversion_count=2) still fires alongside the heat report. Verified: `pytest tests/ -k "manifest or heat"` 49 passed, 1378 deselected in 2.52s; `pyflakes tools/blob_walk_v2/check_render_manifest.py tests/test_check_render_manifest_heat.py` clean. No `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump (additive report in the gate script; no manifest schema change).

- **Manifest heat fields added (M1-B, Patch 2): `walk_driver.py`.** In `_render_direction_tiles`, after each frame's `solved_box` and `trace` are resolved and `render_walk_tile` returns, the manifest is enriched with five new per-tile heat fields: `in_box_hot_mean` (float or null), `in_box_hot_count` (int), `in_box_heat_present` (bool, `count > 0`), `in_box_heat_computed` (bool), `heat_threshold_used` (float). `in_box_heat_computed=True` only when both a live trace with `residual_dog` AND a `solved_box` are present (the primitive actually ran). A COMPUTED-COLD frame records `computed=True, hot_count=0, hot_mean=None, present=False`; a NOT-COMPUTED frame (stub trace from render-only npz, or no solved box) records `computed=False, hot_count=0, hot_mean=None, present=False`. Seed frames are heat-checked the same as non-seed. The threshold is `residual_motion.DEFAULT_THRESHOLD` (10.0, the existing intensity floor), imported once and written as both the primitive argument and `heat_threshold_used` (single source of truth). Two new imports added to `walk_driver.py`: `common_tools.in_box_heat` and `residual_motion`. No change to `walk_render.py`, `check_render_manifest.py`, or any other file. No `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump (additive render-diagnostic fields only). Verified: `pyflakes tools/blob_walk_v2/render/walk_render.py` clean; `pyflakes tools/blob_walk_v2/core/walk_driver.py` clean; `pytest tests/ -k "walk or render or manifest or heat"` 232 passed, 1190 deselected in 2.81s.

- **Docs: manifest heat fields and gate report documented (M1-E, Patch 5): `README.md` + [COORDINATE_SPACES.md](COORDINATE_SPACES.md).** Added "Render manifest heat fields" table (five per-tile fields: `in_box_hot_mean`, `in_box_hot_count`, `in_box_heat_present`, `in_box_heat_computed`, `heat_threshold_used`) and "Heat report in the manifest gate" section to the blob_walk_v2 README. Added `measure_in_box_heat` to the coordinate-spaces table and a short "In-box heat reads PROCESSED arrays with one ROI subtraction" note in COORDINATE_SPACES.md. No code or test changes.

- **In-box motion-cue heat primitive added (M1-A, Patch 1): [in_box_heat.py](../common_tools/in_box_heat.py).** New shared, coordinate-sensitive seam reused by BOTH the track_runner manifest metric (M1-B) and the blob_walk_v2 heat movie (M2-A), so the two callers cannot drift on the box->ROI-array convention (the failure mode behind bug #101). Single pure function `measure_in_box_heat(residual_dog, validity_mask, roi_origin, box, threshold) -> (hot_mean, hot_count)`: validates the typed PROCESSED box with `coord_space.require_processed_box`, subtracts `roi_origin` from the box center EXACTLY ONCE (mirroring `tools/blob_walk_v2/render/walk_draw.py` `processed_box_to_tile_local`), derives float edges from the float center BEFORE any int cast, applies the FIXED rounding rule (floor left/top, floor right/bottom EXCLUSIVE), clamps the half-open region to the array bounds, and selects pixels where `validity_mask != 0 AND residual_dog > threshold`. `hot_mean` is the mean DoG over selected pixels, `hot_count` their number; returns `(None, 0)` when no pixel qualifies. The validity mask is authoritative: an above-threshold but invalid pixel is EXCLUDED even though the residual was already zeroed. The threshold is passed IN by the caller (single source of truth); this module neither hardcodes nor imports it. Placed in `common_tools/` next to `coord_space.py` so both track_runner and tools/blob_walk_v2 import it; logic is NOT added to `coord_space.py` and NOT placed in any `__init__.py`. New sentinel test [test_in_box_heat.py](../tests/test_in_box_heat.py) (6 tests) on a bin=4-shaped array with nonzero `roi_origin (40,80)` and asymmetric box (w != h): correct mean/count of only above-threshold in-box pixels; all-below-threshold -> `(None, 0)`; a fractional center (45.5, 83.5) with NAMED expected pixels pinning the floor/exclusive rule; zero-area and fully-out-of-ROI boxes -> `(None, 0)` no crash; an above-threshold but `validity_mask == 0` pixel EXCLUDED; a `SourceBox` raises `ValueError`. Verified: `pytest tests/ -k heat` 34 passed, 1383 deselected in 2.89s (the 6 new tests included); `pyflakes common_tools/in_box_heat.py tests/test_in_box_heat.py` clean. No `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump (new pure primitive; no on-disk artifact or schema change).

## 2026-05-29

### Additions and New Features

- **Render path processed->tile-local conversion typed (M2 WS2-F, Patch 11): `walk_render.py` + `walk_draw.py`.** Resolved the WS1-A "third coordinate space" open question. TILE-LOCAL (PROCESSED pixels minus the per-tile ROI origin, because the rendered tile is a crop of the processed frame) is kept a RENDER-LOCAL typed helper, NOT a third primitive in `common_tools/coord_space.py`. Rationale: `docs/COORDINATE_SPACES.md` fixes the pipeline at exactly two spaces (SOURCE, PROCESSED), and tile-local is an ephemeral draw-time coordinate parameterized by the ROI origin, valid only inside one tile; adding a third global type mid-migration would both contradict the contract doc and risk destabilizing WS1-A's module that WS2-A..E depend on. New `walk_draw.processed_box_to_tile_local(box: coord_space.ProcessedBox, roi_origin) -> (x1,y1,x2,y2)`: validates the box with `require_processed_box`, subtracts the ROI origin exactly once (THE single conversion site that the render manifest `conversion_count == 1` now refers to), and derives float edges before any rounding (matching `ProcessedBox.edges()` semantics). In `render_walk_tile`, `seed_box`/`solved_box` arrive as PROCESSED dicts from the driver, are built into typed `coord_space.ProcessedBox` at the render entry, validated, and run through the single helper; the resulting edges are drawn by new edge-variant primitives `_draw_torso_box_solid_heavy_edges` (seed: green solid heavy) and `_draw_torso_box_dashed_normal_edges` (solved: cyan dashed normal). The old center-based `_draw_torso_box_solid_heavy` / `_draw_torso_box_dashed_normal` (which recomputed edges inline) were deleted as now-dead. Visual output is byte-identical: same colors, line styles, thickness tiers, rounding policy, and the acceptance box (amber dashed) and ellipse drawing are untouched. Manifest fields unchanged (`box_coord_space_before_draw="processed"`, `conversion_count=1`); their comments now name the single helper. New focused test `test_blob_walk_v2_tile_local.py`: at bin=4 with nonzero asymmetric ROI(37,91) and `w != h`, tile-local edges == PROCESSED edges minus the ROI origin (single subtraction, also pinned in closed form so a transpose surfaces); single-subtract != double-subtract; and a `SourceBox` handed to the helper raises `ValueError`. Verified: pyflakes clean on both edited files + the new test (no output); `pytest tests/ -k "blob_walk_v2 or coord_space or render or manifest or tile_local"` 199 passed, 1195 deselected in 4.36s; `check_render_manifest.py` PASS on an existing 44-tile manifest (`conversion_count == 1` holds). No `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump (in-memory typing/clarity change; PNG pixels and manifest schema unchanged).

- **`state_io` SOURCE boundary typed (M2 WS2-E, Patch 10): [state_io.py](../track_runner/state_io.py).** Added three items to make the npz SOURCE-space boundary explicit and enforced. (1) `_extract_source_box_coords(frame_obj)`: helper called by `write_torso_box_coords` for every frame object in forward/backward/blended paths; accepts either a `coord_space.SourceBox` typed primitive (validated via `require_source_box`, raises `ValueError` on any other typed primitive such as `ProcessedBox`) or a plain dict assumed SOURCE. Typed ProcessedBox passed to `write_torso_box_coords` now raises loud instead of silently writing processed coordinates as source. The internal numpy path (`_round_clip_uint16`, uint16 cast, rounding, clip) is completely unchanged; npz bytes/dtype/rounding are identical. (2) `frame_dict_to_source_box(frame_dict)`: public accessor for callers loading from `load_torso_box_coords`; wraps a plain `{cx,cy,w,h}` frame dict as a `coord_space.SourceBox` so downstream callers can pass it to coord_space conversions (e.g. `.to_processed(geometry)`) without re-introducing implicit space tracking. (3) Added docstring notes to both `write_torso_box_coords` and `load_torso_box_coords` stating SOURCE-space contract and pointing to `docs/COORDINATE_SPACES.md` and `frame_dict_to_source_box`. `SeedsView` unchanged. `import common_tools.coord_space as coord_space` added to local-repo-modules import block. Verified: pyflakes clean (no output); `pytest tests/test_tr_state_io.py` 13 passed in 0.91s (round-trip byte-equivalent); `pytest tests/ -k "state_io or coord_space"` 56 passed in 3.64s. `SCHEMA_VERSION` unchanged (in-memory typing change only; on-disk npz format unchanged).

- **`coord_space` unit and sentinel tests added (M1 WS1-C, Patch 5): [test_coord_space.py](../tests/test_coord_space.py).** 28 tests covering 6 behavioral properties. (1) Round-trip identity `SourcePoint`/`SourceBox` -> `to_processed` -> `to_source` within `bin_factor` tolerance at bin=1/2/4, using the real `_resolve_frame_geometry` path (goodbox snap exercised) with realistic source dims 2816x1584 and 3840x2160. (2) `edges()` midpoint and span relationship: asserts `(x1+x2)/2 == cx`, `(y1+y2)/2 == cy`, `x2-x1 == w`, `y2-y1 == h` in float -- no hardcoded pixel constants. (3) `in_bounds` predicate at the goodbox edge: just inside is True; at/past `processed_width` or `processed_height` is False; negative is False; `ProcessedBox.in_bounds` delegates to center. (4) Double-conversion is unrepresentable: asserts `hasattr(ProcessedPoint(...), "to_processed") is False` and `hasattr(SourcePoint(...), "to_source") is False` (and box equivalents) -- the core API-shape contract. (5) `require_*` guards: each returns the object unchanged for the correct type and raises `ValueError` for the wrong space. (6) bin=4 asymmetric sentinel (mirroring `test_blob_walk_v2_coord_sentinel.py` spirit): source 3840x2160, bin=4, `cx=213, cy=157, w=46, h=74` (center not divisible by 4, w != h); asserts round-trip within tolerance, x-span derives from w (narrow) not h (tall), pure-scale (not double-scale) on `ProcessedBox -> SourceBox`. Any transpose, double-conversion, or axis-swap bug is detectable with these asymmetric values. pyflakes clean; 28/28 passed in 0.11s; no `coord_space.py` defect found.

- **Typed coordinate primitives added (M1 WS1-A): [coord_space.py](../common_tools/coord_space.py).** Four frozen dataclasses encode the two pixel spaces in the Python TYPE so a space mismatch becomes a loud mechanical error rather than a silent numeric bug (root cause of bug #101, where the walker had two callers feeding different spaces and bin>1 source coords built a degenerate ROI). `SourcePoint`/`ProcessedPoint` (cx, cy) and `SourceBox`/`ProcessedBox` (cx, cy, w, h, center-size). Conversions route through `FrameGeometry` and exist only in the legal direction: `SourcePoint.to_processed`, `ProcessedPoint.to_source`, `SourceBox.to_processed`, `ProcessedBox.to_source`. The wrong direction is method-not-found (`ProcessedPoint(...).to_processed` raises AttributeError), so double conversion is unrepresentable and caught by pyflakes. Conversion stays PURE SCALE (never clamped); off-frame is a separate explicit predicate `ProcessedPoint.in_bounds` / `ProcessedBox.in_bounds` (center in `[state_io.py](../track_runner/state_io.py).** Option A coord-system robustness (2026-05-29): source-of-truth seeds remain in source-pixel coords; `SeedsView` wraps a seeds dict and exposes processed-pixel coords lazily via the held `FrameGeometry` reference. `assert_geometry_match(geometry)` raises `RuntimeError` loudly when `bin_factor` differs. `view.source` accessor returns the original dict unchanged. `view.seeds` is lazy-cached. Prevents coord-system ambiguity at the walker/observer boundary by making the conversion a single load-time operation rather than a per-call convention.
- **`walk_io.load_walker_seeds_view(video_basename, geometry)` added to `walk_io.py`.** Thin wrapper around `state_io.load_seeds_view`; provides the canonical entry point for the walker pipeline to obtain processed-pixel seeds. `load_walker_seeds` (source-pixel legacy API) preserved for diagnostic tools.
- **3 new test files for Option A implementation:**
  - [test_seeds_view.py](../tests/test_seeds_view.py): 5 tests covering `SeedsView` at bin=1 and bin=4, geometry mismatch guard, source accessor immutability, and lazy cache. All pass.
  - [test_observe_blob_at_processed_contract.py](../tests/test_observe_blob_at_processed_contract.py): 3 tests verifying `roi_override` and `dog_diameter_override` passed as processed-pixel coords are used as-is (no internal conversion). All pass.
  - [test_walker_bin_consistency.py](../tests/test_walker_bin_consistency.py): 4 regression tests for bugs #1 (WARP_SCALE_MISMATCH), #2 (override source-scale), #3 (ROI_CLAMP_SPACE_MISMATCH), and #4 (SeedsView bin_factor mismatch guard). All pass.

- **Walker now solves a per-frame torso-box path (WS1-A): [walk_walker.py](../track_runner/blob_walk/walk_walker.py) + [walk_status.py](../track_runner/blob_walk/walk_status.py) emit `WalkSummary.direction_path`, a list of processed-space `{cx, cy, w, h, conf}` boxes.** The walker solves POSITION; box SIZE is seed-derived geometry (the blob does not drive size). Per-frame `w`/`h` are linearly interpolated between the two bracketing seed torso boxes, held constant from the nearest anchor past the interval ends (and across the pre-race range per contract C4). `torso_h_px` is no longer `None` for step>0. `conf` is a deterministic distance-from-anchor decay mirroring `velocity_model` (0.97 decay, 0.1 floor, 1.0 at the anchor), with no status-tier fork. FWD and BWD passes remain independent (C9). New test file `test_blob_walk_v2_box_path.py`.
- **Walker now persists a solved source-frame torso-box npz per video (WS1-B): `walk_driver.py` assembles `solved_intervals` keyed by [interval_fingerprint.py](../track_runner/interval_fingerprint.py) `compute_interval_fingerprint`, blends FWD/BWD via [interval_solver.py](../track_runner/interval_solver.py) `blend_paths`, projects processed->source via `reader.geometry.processed_to_source` (and `processed_to_source_delta` for w/h), and writes per video to `tr_config/{stem}.track_runner.torso_box_coords.npz` via [state_io.py](../track_runner/state_io.py) `write_torso_box_coords`.** The write is a load-merge-write-once aggregate per video (one shared npz keyed by interval fingerprint). Neighbor seed `w`/`h` are wired through for the size interpolation in WS1-A. Walker output intentionally replaces any existing solved interval for the same seed-pair key.
- **Single coordinate-space contract doc created (typed-coord M1 / WS1-B): [COORDINATE_SPACES.md](COORDINATE_SPACES.md).** States the two spaces (SOURCE = full-frame, npz storage via `state_io`, encoder consumes; PROCESSED = post-bin + goodbox snap, where the walker decodes/steps/draws, `reader.width`/`height`); that `source == processed` only at `bin_factor == 1`; the goodbox subtlety (`processed_width` can be `< source//bin_factor`, so pure-scale `source_to_processed` can land out-of-frame, which is why `coord_space.ProcessedPoint.in_bounds` exists); who lives in which space (driver seeds / walker stepping / `observe_blob_at` INPUTS = PROCESSED, `observe_blob_at` RETURN centroid = SOURCE -- called out as an explicit asymmetry that M2/WS2-B types; `SeedsView.source` vs `.seeds`; npz = SOURCE); the enforcement mechanism (typed primitives in [coord_space.py](../common_tools/coord_space.py), type == space, double-conversion is method-not-found, wrong-space fails loud via `require_*`); and the #101 lesson (two walker callers fed different spaces -- `make_walk_html_v2.process_video` SOURCE vs `walk_driver.main` PROCESSED -- so at `bin>1` the source seed built a degenerate ROI; the typed boundary makes this a boundary error). Cross-links C2 (torso units) and C13 (npz frame data) in [TRACK_RUNNER_CONTRACT.md](TRACK_RUNNER_CONTRACT.md) and [TRACK_RUNNER_DESIGN.md](TRACK_RUNNER_DESIGN.md). Also deleted the stale "model B" coordinate comment from [frame_reader.py](../common_tools/frame_reader.py) and replaced it with a short pointer to this doc; conversion method bodies are unchanged (pure scale, bounds via `in_bounds`).
- **New machine-checkable render-manifest gate (WS2-C): `check_render_manifest.py`.** Fails on any non-seed frame lacking a solved box or on any tile with `conversion_count != 1`. A `render_manifest.json` is written per video. New test file `test_blob_walk_v2_coord_sentinel.py` drives an asymmetric `bin=4` / ROI(37,91) / `w != h` sentinel through the full processed->source->npz->reload->tile path (4/4 pass).

### Behavior or Interface Changes

- **`velocity_model.py` `_apply_blob_snap` call sites typed (M2 WS2-C2): [velocity_model.py](../track_runner/velocity_model.py).** Two `observe_blob_at` call sites (formerly passing bare `(raw_cx, raw_cy)` tuple for `pred_center` and `(w, h)` for `pred_box`) now pass typed `coord_space.ProcessedPoint(cx=raw_cx, cy=raw_cy)` and `coord_space.ProcessedBox(cx=raw_cx, cy=raw_cy, w=w, h=h)`. The unpack `bx, by = observation.center_pixel` (line ~831) replaced with `obs_processed = observation.center_pixel.to_processed(effective_reader.geometry); bx, by = obs_processed.cx, obs_processed.cy`. Space finding: `raw_cx`/`raw_cy`/`w`/`h` are produced by `_compute_raw_pred_forward`/`_compute_raw_pred_backward` via `scene_transform.scene_box_to_pixel`, which maps scene coords back to the reader's decoded-frame pixel space = PROCESSED at any bin_factor. `bx`/`by` are compared to `raw_cx`/`raw_cy` (PROCESSED) in the proximity gate, so the source point returned from `observe_blob_at` is converted to PROCESSED via `.to_processed(reader.geometry)` before extraction. At bin_factor==1 (production solve path), source==processed so both conversions are numeric identity -- no behavior change. `import common_tools.coord_space as coord_space` added to the local-repo-modules import block. Verified: pyflakes clean (no output); `pytest tests/ -k "velocity or hermite or interval or residual or coord_space"` -> 202 passed in 1.78s.

- **Reorganized `tools/blob_walk_v2/` into `core/` and `render/` subdirs via `git mv`.** `core/` holds the walk orchestration, algorithm, I/O, and schema modules (`walk_driver.py`, [walk_walker.py](../track_runner/blob_walk/walk_walker.py), [walk_viterbi.py](../track_runner/blob_walk/walk_viterbi.py), [walk_status.py](../track_runner/blob_walk/walk_status.py), [walk_motion_gate.py](../track_runner/blob_walk/walk_motion_gate.py), `walk_io.py`, [walk_debug_log.py](../track_runner/blob_walk/walk_debug_log.py)); `render/` holds the tile and HTML output modules (`walk_render.py`, `walk_draw.py`, `walk_palette.py`, `walk_html.py`). The entry script `make_walk_html_v2.py`, `walk_paths.py`, `walk_util.py`, `__init__.py`, and `README.md` stay at the package root. `walk_paths.setup()` now also inserts the `core/` and `render/` subdirs onto `sys.path`, so every module keeps resolving its siblings by bare name unchanged. `walk_walker.py` and `walk_io.py` dropped their hand-rolled `__file__`-depth path blocks (which would have computed the wrong repo root from a subdir) and now route through `walk_paths.setup()` like the render modules already did; `walk_io.py` captures its `_REPO_ROOT` from `setup()`'s return value. `core/walk_driver.py` gained a standalone-run bootstrap that puts the package root on `sys.path` before `import walk_paths` so `python3 tools/blob_walk_v2/core/walk_driver.py` still works. The e2e harness and the kept unit tests now bootstrap through `walk_paths.setup()` so `core/` and `render/` are reachable; `test_blob_walk_v2_no_hermite.py` switched to `rglob` so the C6 import scan covers the moved files. Byte-identical: the `e2e_blob_walk_baseline.sh` gate passes (224 verdict rows), no schema change.
- **`make_walk_html_v2.py` is now the documented blob_walk_v2 entry point.** The redundant `blob_walk_v2_cli.py` subcommand wrapper (which had collapsed to only `render` plus an `all` alias, both subprocess-proxying `make_walk_html_v2.py`) was removed. `make_walk_html_v2.py` is the all-in-one driver: it discovers videos, optionally walks intervals (`-w`/`--walk`), renders walker tiles, and builds `walk.html`, delegating per-interval work to `walk_driver.run_interval_walk`. Its argparse already exposed every flag the old `render` subcommand proxied (`-o`/`--output-root`, `--intervals-from-corpus`, `-j`/`--workers`, `-w`/`--walk`), so no user-facing capability was lost. `README.md` "Entry point" section rewritten to document `python3 tools/blob_walk_v2/make_walk_html_v2.py [flags]`; the subcommand table was replaced with the driver's actual key flags. No walker or render behavior change.
- **Corpus quality metrics now render inside `walk.html`; the standalone scorer is gone.** `walk_html.py` gains a corpus-level "Quality summary" section at the top (classification counts: no_signal / completed_low_quality / completed_normal; median accepted_fraction FWD/BWD/combined over non-no_signal intervals; median FWD/BWD agreement; plus a per-video rollup table) and a per-interval row showing each interval's `classification` and FWD/BWD agreement (median px). Classification thresholds match the former scorer (no_signal when both accepted_fraction < 0.1; completed_low_quality when either < 0.3). The agreement metric reads each pass's own accepted positions independently (contract C9) and is reported as raw px for diagnostics (allowed under C2). Presentation-only: the walker, the verdict CSV schema, `walk_debug_log.SCHEMA_VERSION`, and all per-frame computation are unchanged; the `e2e_blob_walk_baseline.sh` gate passes byte-for-byte. Reason: consolidating the quality readout into the single `walk.html` artifact per user direction.

- **`README.md` updated with full script use-case and liveness map.** "Standalone scripts" section now includes a table linking each script to its CLI subcommand. New "Diagnostic scripts" section documents `compare_walker_vs_hermite.py` (live, has unit tests, run manually) and `sweep_radius.py` (probably stale design-phase one-off, no tests, no CLI integration). "Library modules" section expanded to include `walk_driver.py` (critical orchestrator used by e2e tests, has an unused `main()` that is not a CLI entry point), `walk_paths.py`, and `replay_models.py`.

- **`observe_blob_at` in [residual_motion.py](../track_runner/residual_motion.py): `roi_override`, `dog_diameter_override`, `pred_center`, `pred_box`, and `acceptance_box` now expected in PROCESSED-pixel coords.** Prior Model B contract ("source coords everywhere, converted at this boundary") retired after three auto-bin coord defects. Conversion block removed from the entry block. At `bin_factor=1`, source==processed, so existing solve-mode callers (`velocity_model.py`) are unaffected. Walker callers now pass processed coords from `SeedsView.seeds`.
- **`observe_blob_at` boundary is now TYPED (M2 / WS2-B): [residual_motion.py](../track_runner/residual_motion.py).** The processed-in / source-out asymmetry that the prior Option A bullet left as bare tuples is now expressed with `coord_space` primitives so the seam fails loud instead of silently feeding a SOURCE centroid back into a PROCESSED stepping loop. New signature: `pred_center: coord_space.ProcessedPoint`, `pred_box: coord_space.ProcessedBox` (only `.w`/`.h` used; center ignored), `roi_override: coord_space.ProcessedBox | None`, `acceptance_box: coord_space.ProcessedBox | None`; `dog_diameter_override` stays a bare scalar (a LENGTH in processed pixels, not a point). At entry, `require_processed_point` / `require_processed_box` guards raise `ValueError` immediately when handed a SOURCE-space primitive -- this is exactly what would have caught bug #101 at the boundary (verified: handing `SourcePoint` raises "expected ProcessedPoint (PROCESSED space), got SourcePoint"). The RETURN is now typed: `BlobObservation.center_pixel` is a `coord_space.SourcePoint` (was a bare `(cx, cy)` tuple). Return-type decision resolved in favor of "return typed SourcePoint" (per the plan's open decision): the walker cannot feed it back as a prediction without an explicit `.to_processed(geometry)`, which makes the space-flip mechanical rather than convention-bound. Internal math is unchanged: typed inputs are unwrapped to floats right after the guards (`roi_override`/`acceptance_box` via `.edges()`), and the single processed->source conversion happens once at the exit (`ProcessedPoint(winner).to_source(geometry)` at `bin_factor != 1`, else a direct `SourcePoint` wrap -- byte-identical to the prior `geometry.processed_to_source` path). The trace artifact is unchanged: `BlobObserverTrace.acceptance_box` still stores a processed `(x1,y1,x2,y2)` tuple (derived via `.edges()` at the single trace site), and a stale source->processed conversion remnant in the trace `in_acceptance_box` computation was deleted (acceptance_box is already processed). No `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump (call-shape change, not a stored-artifact change). Observe-related unit tests migrated to typed inputs: [test_blob_observation_contract.py](../tests/test_blob_observation_contract.py), [test_tr_residual_motion_window.py](../tests/test_tr_residual_motion_window.py), [test_tr_residual_motion_bin.py](../tests/test_tr_residual_motion_bin.py), [test_observe_blob_at_processed_contract.py](../tests/test_observe_blob_at_processed_contract.py), [test_walker_bin_consistency.py](../tests/test_walker_bin_consistency.py).
