- **New fixture-driven pytest `test_alternate_blob_winner.py` for WP-2B.** Two tests on a one-row in-memory `verdicts.csv` fixture (`lost_at_stage == "gate_prox"`, chosen winner at `dist_h = 1.5`, alternate corridor blob at `dist_h = 0.4`). Asserts the alternate-blob rescue check fires (per-interval rescue_rate == 1.0) and the corpus rollup decides H_ALT_PASSES = SUPPORTED. The tool module is loaded via `importlib.util.spec_from_file_location` since `tools/` is not on `sys.path`. Pyflakes clean; 2/2 GREEN.
- **New fixture-driven pytest `test_blob_fix_suspension_check.py` for WP-1D.** Three tests on a hand-built tmp_path `oracle/HYPOTHESIS_TESTS.json` fixture matching the WP-1A sidecar schema (`corpus.h2.{verdict,rate,n_eligible}` and `per_video.<video>.h2.{verdict,rate,n_eligible}` for 12 simulated videos). Covers the REFUTED-corpus PROCEED path (corpus rate `0.05`, all per-video rates below `0.30` -> `H_RAW_PRED_CORPUS: REFUTED`, `Overall: PROCEED`), the SUPPORTED-corpus + 6/12-per-video PLAN_SUSPENDED path (corpus rate `0.42`, six videos at `rate >= 0.30` -> `H_RAW_PRED_CORPUS: SUPPORTED`, `Overall: PLAN_SUSPENDED`), and the quorum-boundary case (corpus SUPPORTED but only 5/12 videos at the 30% threshold -> `H_RAW_PRED_CORPUS: INCONCLUSIVE`, `Overall: PROCEED`). Tests assert on the canonical verdict lines (`H_RAW_PRED_CORPUS:` and `Overall:`), not on heading levels or prose, so the suite survives cosmetic Markdown changes. Pyflakes clean; 3/3 GREEN.
- **New fixture-driven pytest `test_centroid_bias.py` for WP-2C.** Five tests on a two-row in-memory oracle.csv fixture (one TRUST 0% row with `overlap_frac=0.7` and `dist_h=0.8`, one positive-control row with a known shift-helps geometry). Asserts the cross-tab bucket assignment lands the H1 hit in `overlap_high_far`, the AC-2d mean-delta computes to -50 px (PASS) on TRUST 0%, the AC-2e mean-delta computes to -30 px (PASS) on positive controls, the sidecar JSON ships the documented five-key schema with correct types, and `policy_e_eligible` short-circuits to False when the per-video quorum cannot be met. Pyflakes clean; 5/5 GREEN.
- **New import-boundary pytest `test_counterfactual_gate_sim_imports.py` for WP-3A.** Three tests mirroring `tests/test_visualize_blob_gates_imports.py`. AST-parses `tools/counterfactual_gate_sim.py` and asserts the import list (1) includes `track_runner.velocity_model` (REQUIRED, so AC-3b byte-fidelity stays bound to the live production constants), (2) does NOT include `track_runner.residual_motion` (DENYLISTED per AC-3l; observer logic must be read from verdicts.csv, never re-implemented). Third test loads the tool via `importlib.util.spec_from_file_location` and asserts the module-level `POLICIES` set equals the plan default `{"A", "B", "C", "F", "G"}` (policy E is conditional and joins only when `policy_e_eligible` is true). Pyflakes clean; 3/3 GREEN.
- **New fixture-driven pytest `test_per_video_report.py` for WP-1C.** Five tests on a hand-built tmp_path run-root layout (`m2_sweep/per_video_selection.csv` with one ranking-sample TRUST 0% row; `m2_sweep/<video>/interval_<s>_<e>/verdicts.csv` with 10 prox-rejected rows + 2 accepted rows carrying a JSON `raw_box` column with drifting `h`; minimal `CLASSIFICATION.md`; `oracle/HYPOTHESIS_TESTS.json` with H1 supported and H2/H6/H8 below 30%). Asserts the per-video `<video>.md` exists with all expected section headings (Header, TRUST 0%, Positive-control, Mixed, winner_dist_h histogram, Hypothesis verdicts, Torso-h jitter), the interval id appears under TRUST 0%, `corpus_index.md` exists and contains a Markdown link to the per-video file, `per_video_summary.csv` exists with the documented column set and the fixture video row reports `trust_0_count == 1`, `h1_supported == 1`, and `h2/h6/h8_supported == 0`, torso-h jitter is a finite value on the fixture (raw_box column parses cleanly), and `winner_dist_h_mode_bin_low` falls inside `[0.0, 3.0)`. Pyflakes clean; 5/5 GREEN.

## 2026-05-06

### Fixes and Maintenance

- **Code-review follow-ups on the V1 ceiling-stabilizer harness.** `tools/analyze_torso_box_noise.py`: dropped the `try/finally` resource-cleanup block in `encode_review_clips` (cv2.VideoCapture and video_io.VideoWriter both release on garbage collection; explicit close at end of loop is enough); switched `_draw_torso_box_on_panel` from `state.get(key)` to `state[key]` so missing torso fields fail loudly instead of silently skipping the draw; promoted the nested `_stage_log_arrays` and `_bucket` closures inside `write_stage_trace_csv` and `_format_fit_diagnostics_section` to module-level helpers `_stage_log_arrays` and `_run_length_bucket`; corrected stale `--encode-review` help text (now says `source_clip.mkv`, `pre_v1_clip.mkv`, `v1_clip.mkv`, `side_by_side.mkv` matching the three-panel layout). `track_runner/tr_crop.py`: added `_use_rolling_min_ceiling` to the `direct_center_crop_trajectory` docstring `Args:` block, calling out the kwarg as a diagnostic harness hook only. New test `test_tr_crop_production_v1_reduces_bounce_vs_legacy` calls `direct_center_crop_trajectory` directly with `_use_rolling_min_ceiling=True` and `=False` to assert V1's bounce-suppression on the production code path (not just the tool simulation). `output_smoke/zoom_bounce/EVIDENCE/v1_ceiling_aware_s4/V1_RESULTS.md` gained a per-video boundary-violation count table (zero violations across Glenbrook, IMG_3823, IMG_3830). Test count goes from 24 to 25; pyflakes clean.

### Additions and New Features

- **New module [off_frame_geometry.py](../track_runner/off_frame_geometry.py).** Pure-function geometry library for NIF (not-in-frame) crop synthesis. Public API: `NifSpan` dataclass (`start_frame`, `end_frame`, `exit_side`, `before_frame`, `after_frame`, `stable_w`, `stable_h`, `anchor_start`, `anchor_end`); `build_nif_spans(seeds, solved_trajectory, frame_size, race_start_frame)`; `infer_exit_side(last_visible_box, frame_size)` (box-edge-distance rule, left/right preferred within 5%); `build_off_frame_states(nif_spans, frame_size)` returning per-frame `(cx, cy, w, h)` uint16-safe tuples. Raises `ValueError` if any `not_in_frame` seed lies in `[test_off_frame_geometry.py](../tests/test_off_frame_geometry.py).** Nine behavioral tests on synthetic numpy fixtures (no video I/O): right-edge inference and `cx == frame_width` fill; mirrored left-edge case; size as integer mean of bracketing boxes; linear cy interpolation at midpoint; single-bracket open-ended span; vertical-exit fallback (`exit_side == "bottom"`); 5%-tie-favor rule selecting left/right over top/bottom; `uint16` range invariant; pre-race NIF raises `ValueError` with the verbatim contract message. Patch 2 of `~/.claude/plans/atomic-jingling-treasure.md` (WP-A2).

### Behavior or Interface Changes

- **[cli.py](../track_runner/cli.py) wires encode-time NIF synthesis (around line 2218).** After loading seeds and the solved trajectory, the encode path now calls `build_nif_spans` followed by `build_off_frame_states` and constructs an in-memory derived trajectory in which NIF-span frames carry edge-anchored `(cx, cy, w, h)`. The bracketing visible seed frames remain normal in-frame geometry. The on-disk `tr_config/{basename}.track_runner.torso_box_coords.npz` is intentionally NOT modified; [state_io.py](../track_runner/state_io.py) and [interval_solver.py](../track_runner/interval_solver.py) are intentionally NOT modified. Pre-race NIF aborts the encode run via the `ValueError` from `build_nif_spans`. Patch 3 of `~/.claude/plans/atomic-jingling-treasure.md` (WP-B1).

- **[tr_crop.py](../track_runner/tr_crop.py) `trajectory_to_crop_rects` and `direct_center_crop_trajectory` accept `nif_frames: set`.** Frames in the set are treated as authoritative calculated crop geometry: `cx, cy, w, h` are read directly with `conf=1.0` and `source="nif_edge_anchor"`. The `hold_last` branch is bypassed for NIF frames; NIF frames are not weak fallback frames. Without `nif_frames` (legacy callers), behavior is byte-equal to today. Patch 4 of `~/.claude/plans/atomic-jingling-treasure.md` (WP-C1).

- **[encoder.py](../track_runner/encoder.py) overlays NIF frames with dashed inferred-position style.** On NIF frames, the runner-box overlay calls `draw_dashed_rect` instead of solid-rectangle rendering, matching the visual encoding principle that dashed lines convey inferred geometry. Box may render partially or entirely outside the visible frame because the box is centered at the edge anchor; the existing renderer clips. No new style colors. Patch 5 of `~/.claude/plans/atomic-jingling-treasure.md` (WP-C2).

- **Updated [TRACK_RUNNER_DESIGN.md](TRACK_RUNNER_DESIGN.md) "Trajectory erasure philosophy" section.** Replaces the previous "erase the gap" framing for NIF with "fill the gap with edge-anchored geometry, NIF-context provided in-memory" framing, and links to the new contract doc. Approximate-seed erasure language is retained for that case. Patch 6 of `~/.claude/plans/atomic-jingling-treasure.md` (WP-D1).

### Additions and New Features

- **New doc [OFF_FRAME_GEOMETRY.md](OFF_FRAME_GEOMETRY.md).** Captures the NIF storage and interpretation contract: encode-time derived view (no schema bump, no `SCHEMA_VERSION` change, no new per-frame fields, no dtype change on `cx, cy`); the four edge-anchored cases (right `cx=frame_width`, left `cx=0`, bottom `cy=frame_height`, top `cy=0`); edge-pinned values are not sentinels outside NIF context; crop confidence is `conf=1.0` with `source="nif_edge_anchor"`; `NifSpan` list is the in-memory semantic object of record and is not persisted; pre-race NIF is unsupported with a verbatim `ValueError` message; exit side is inferred from box-edge distances with a 5% left/right tie-favor; cross-references to contracts C1, C2, C4, C5, C12, C13. [state_io.py](../track_runner/state_io.py), [interval_solver.py](../track_runner/interval_solver.py), and `torso_box_coords.npz` were intentionally NOT modified. Patch 6 of `~/.claude/plans/atomic-jingling-treasure.md` (WP-D1).

## 2026-05-05

### Additions and New Features

- **`tools/analyze_torso_box_noise.py` gains fit-to-source diagnostics for the V1 ceiling-stabilizer plan.** `compute_pre_encode_crop_signals` now also returns a `fit_diagnostics` dict per frame: `requested_h`, `fitted_h`, `shrink_h`, `geometric_ceiling_h` (the bouncy signal V1 attacks), `s4_overshoot` (informational only -- S4-EMA over-shoot of S3 was confirmed mathematically irrelevant; production S5 already clips to the same ceiling), `limiting_edge_code` (0=none, 1=top, 2=bottom, 3=left, 4=right), distance to each frame edge, edge-clamp `run_id` and `run_length`. The "Fit-to-source diagnostics" section in `stage_bisect.md` reports `hotspot_severity(geometric_ceiling)`, run-length histogram, per-edge fractions, and Pearson rho between the ceiling oscillation and S5-only log-velocity. Diagnostic done-check fired PASS on all three representative videos (Glenbrook rho=+0.991, ceiling hotspot=8.40; IMG_3823 rho=+0.718; IMG_3830 hotspot=0.566). Patch 1 of `~/.claude/plans/lucky-napping-lake.md` (WP-A1, WP-A2).

- **`tools/analyze_torso_box_noise.py` gains a `--variant v1` mode that simulates the rolling-min source-fit ceiling.** New helpers `compute_v1_rolling_min_ceiling(signals, video_info, cfg, window)` and `rolling_min_1d` produce three new stage outputs (`S3_v1`, `S4_v1`, `S5_v1`) by smoothing the per-frame geometric ceiling with a centered rolling minimum and using the smoothed ceiling at S3 and S5 clip points. Three invariants are asserted in the helper: smooth_ceiling <= geometric_ceiling per frame; `S5_v1_w` <= max(smooth_ceiling_w, 1.0); `S5_v1_w` <= max(production S5_w, 1.0). With `--variant v1`, the per-stage table gains rows for S3_v1, S5_v1, S3_v1b (window 3, informational), S5_v1b; the trace CSV gains the corresponding columns plus `smooth_ceiling_w`. Default `--variant none` is byte-identical to today. Patch 2 of `~/.claude/plans/lucky-napping-lake.md` (WP-B1, WP-B2).

- **PENDING-PRODUCTION: `track_runner/tr_crop.py` ceiling-stabilized fit-to-source (Patch 4 prepared, not merged).** New helper `_rolling_min_ceiling_per_frame(cx_arr, cy_arr, frame_w, frame_h, aspect_ratio, window=7)` computes the centered rolling minimum of the per-frame geometric source-fit ceiling. In `direct_center_crop_trajectory`, the per-frame `_max_centered_fit_size` calls inside the `fit_to_source` block (S3 first fit and S5 second fit) are replaced with vectorized `numpy.minimum(smoothed_w, smooth_ceiling_w)` clamps using the smoothed ceiling. Window length is fixed at 7 in code; no config knob, no CLI flag, no help-text change per the user's "no new flags" directive. `_max_centered_fit_size` is not modified (other callers may exist). Tool-side `compute_v1_rolling_min_ceiling` now delegates to the production helper -- single source of truth. Two new production tests: `test_tr_crop_rolling_min_ceiling_helper_lower_bound` (sawtooth-cx fixture; smoothed <= geometric per frame) and `test_tr_crop_production_uses_smoothed_ceiling` (regression check that `direct_center_crop_trajectory` output never exceeds the geometric ceiling). Test count goes from 22 to 24; pyflakes clean. Verification: re-running the analyze tool with `--variant v1` after the refactor reproduces the pre-refactor V1 metrics byte-for-byte on all three videos (Glenbrook S5_v1 hotspot 0.945489 unchanged; IMG_3823 0.382080 unchanged; IMG_3830 0.275618 unchanged). Patches 4-5 of `~/.claude/plans/lucky-napping-lake.md` (WP-D1, WP-D2). **Not merged yet.** Blocked on user visual review of `output_smoke/zoom_bounce/EVIDENCE/v1_ceiling_aware_s4/REVIEW_CLIPS.md` (gate C-4) and architect approval of the unflagged production behavior change. See "Production patch prepared" section in `output_smoke/zoom_bounce/EVIDENCE/v1_ceiling_aware_s4/V1_RESULTS.md`.

- **`tools/analyze_torso_box_noise.py` gains `--encode-review` for the V1 visual review.** New helper `encode_review_clips(source_path, signals, variant_stages, video_info, output_dir)` builds per-frame production and V1 crop rects across a 30 s slice centered on the worst S5 hotspot frame, applies the rects via `tr_crop.apply_crop`, and emits three .mkv files (`production_clip.mkv`, `v1_clip.mkv`, `side_by_side.mkv` -- 1080x1080, 2160x1080 hstack) using `track_runner.video_io.VideoWriter`. No production-code change; the harness reuses existing primitives. Diagnostic surface only -- the encoder CLI / help text are unchanged. Patch 3 (WP-C3) of `~/.claude/plans/lucky-napping-lake.md`.

- **New evidence document `REVIEW_CLIPS.md`.** Side-by-side production vs V1 (rolling-min source-fit ceiling, window 7) clips for the three representative videos centered on the worst S5 hotspot per video. Glenbrook (60 fps, primary fix target): center frame 14913, range `V1_RESULTS.md`.** Per-video stage tables for production vs V1 (window 7) vs V1b (window 3) on Glenbrook, IMG_3823, IMG_3830. **V1 reduces S5 hotspot severity by -91.6% on Glenbrook (11.25 -> 0.95), -78.7% on IMG_3823 (1.80 -> 0.38), and -0.1% on IMG_3830 (essentially unchanged because only 0.17% of frames are edge-clamped on that clip).** S3 hotspot drops are even more dramatic (-92.5% Glenbrook, -86.4% IMG_3823). `final_crop_log_velocity_p95` does not worsen on any video (max change -0.14%). S4-to-S5 amplification ratio drops from 13.95x to 1.17x on Glenbrook and from 5.01x to 1.07x on IMG_3823. Window 7 dramatically beats window 3 on Glenbrook (V1 hotspot 0.95 vs V1b hotspot 8.05); equivalent on the small-torso videos. Metric gates C-1, C-2, C-3 all PASS. C-4 (visual framing review on side-by-side encoded clips, WP-C3) is pending user review. C-1 and C-2 confirmed by mathematical reasoning prior to any metric run that the previously-considered "ceiling-aware S4 clamp" variant was a no-op (production S5 already applies `min(input, geometric_ceiling)` per frame, so a second clamp to the same ceiling is identity); the bisect arrow that was falsified is named explicitly per `feedback_falsified_hypothesis_framing.md`. Patch 3 of `~/.claude/plans/lucky-napping-lake.md` (WP-C1, WP-C2; WP-C3 deferred pending user review).

- **`tools/analyze_torso_box_noise.py` gains a `--bisect-stages` mode that traces the zoom-bounce metric through every stage of `tr_crop.direct_center_crop_trajectory`.** `compute_pre_encode_crop_signals` now also returns `S0_raw` (scale-normalized raw torso), `S1_w_h_avg`, `S2_ema_1`, `S3_fit_1`, `S4_ema_2`, `S5_fit_2`, plus `edge_clamped_S3`. New helper `compute_per_stage_zoom_metrics(signals, fps)` returns one row per stage with `log_velocity_p95`, `hotspot_severity`, and `n_frames_finite`. When `--bisect-stages` is set, the tool emits `crop_zoom_stage_trace.csv` (one row per frame, all six stages with `log_size` and `log_velocity` columns plus the two `edge_clamped_*` flags) and `stage_bisect.md` (per-stage table plus the bisect call by the >=50%-of-S5 rule with hotspot-severity / >20%-transition fallback). Production code in `track_runner/tr_crop.py` is read-only; the helper still reuses `tr_crop._forward_backward_ema` and `tr_crop._max_centered_fit_size` directly so semantics cannot drift. Patches 1 and 2 of `~/.claude/plans/lucky-napping-lake.md` (WP-A1, WP-A2, WP-A3).

- **New evidence document `STAGE_BISECT.md`.** Per-stage zoom-bounce tables for the three representative videos (Glenbrook 60 fps, IMG_3823 30 fps, IMG_3830 30 fps), plus a cross-video aggregate. Numerical cross-check: `log_velocity_p95[S5]` matches the parent baseline exactly (0.063148, 0.026464, 0.017571), so the helper refactor preserves S5 byte-for-byte. The bisect identifies `S3_fit_1` (the first fit-to-source pass) as the only stage that amplifies hotspot severity on all three videos (Glenbrook 64x, IMG_3823 7x, IMG_3830 2x); `S5_fit_2` shows the same amplification pattern. The 50%-of-S5 rule alone disagrees across videos because raw torso noise on the small-torso videos (IMG_3823, IMG_3830) is several times the final crop bounce while on Glenbrook it is much smaller; the per-stage tables explain the asymmetry. Recommendation: the next plan should target the fit-to-source step. Patch 5 of `~/.claude/plans/lucky-napping-lake.md` (WP-C2, WP-C3).

- **Archived [STABILIZER_METHOD_COMPARISON.md](archive/STABILIZER_METHOD_COMPARISON.md).** Verbatim copy of the parent stabilizer comparison doc previously living under `output_smoke/zoom_bounce/EVIDENCE/torso_noise/`. The bisect plan and `STAGE_BISECT.md` now link to the archived path so the reference is stable. Patch 4 of `~/.claude/plans/lucky-napping-lake.md` (WP-C1).

- **New library module [torso_size_stabilizer.py](../track_runner/torso_size_stabilizer.py).** Public API `stabilize_torso_size(coords, method, window)` plus internal helpers `median_filter_1d`, `hampel_filter_1d`, `mad_gated_filter_1d`. Operates on the in-memory loaded trajectory (no schema change per C13). Position cx, cy returned byte-equal; only w and h are stabilized (size and position are independent signals per C5). Method choices: `none, median, hampel, mad_gated`; constant-neighborhood handling treats any deviation from a zero-MAD local median as a structural outlier so the filter does not silently pass through one-frame jumps. Patch 2 of `~/.claude/plans/declarative-shimmying-brooks.md` (M2.A stabilizer library).

- **New tool `tools/measure_black_bars.py` measures letterboxing exposure in encoded movies.** Per-frame top, bottom, left, and right border-bar fractions plus aggregates (`frames_with_any_bar`, `mean_bar_area_fraction` over frames-with-any-bar, `max_bar_area_fraction`, per-edge max bar height). Tunable via `-t/--threshold` (default 16 on 0-255 luminance) and `-r/--row-fraction` (default 0.95). Used by the Path A trade-off review: a low score on baseline + a high score on path_a is the artifact-cost the user must accept knowingly. Per-frame area uses inclusion-exclusion to avoid double-counting corners. Wired into the recipe doc as Step 6.

### Behavior or Interface Changes

- **`tools/find_zoom_hotspots.py` default hotspot score replaced.** New default is `velocity_p95` (window-local p95 of `abs(diff(log_scale))`), which separates jitter from slow drift. The legacy `abs_smoothed` score (rolling mean of `abs(log_scale)`) is now selectable via `-s/--score abs_smoothed` and is also emitted as a secondary cross-check column in the hotspot report alongside the primary score. The third option `rms_detrended` (RMS of log_scale after rolling-median detrend) is also available. Drift signals no longer dominate hotspot ranking; this matters because a slow zoom creep registered as a high-intensity "hotspot" under the old metric even though there was no jitter to fix. Reviewer's request 2026-05-05.
- **`velocity_p95` filters Fourier-Mellin clamp-saturated samples.** The Fourier-Mellin scale estimate in `assess_pixel_zoom.estimate_scale_fourier_mellin` is clamped to roughly `[0.80, 1.25]`, so log_scale is in `[-0.223, +0.223]`. Frames where the FFT estimator hit the clamp boundary (typically black-frame transitions, scene cuts, or motion-blurred pre-race static frames) produce velocity values up to `2 * 0.223 = 0.446` that are not real bounce. Without filtering, baseline runs against IMG_3627 produced a saturated tie at 0.446287 across the first five hotspot windows, drowning out any real-bounce ranking. The score now masks velocity samples whose endpoints have `|log_scale| >= 0.20` (constant `LOG_SCALE_CLAMP_THRESHOLD` in `find_zoom_hotspots.py`) and uses `nanpercentile` so saturated windows do not dominate. Discovered while executing Milestone 3a baseline diagnostic.
- **`tools/correlate_bounce_with_edge.py` adds frame-alignment + lagged Spearman.** New "Frame alignment" stanza prints `n_video_frames`, `n_trajectory_frames`, `fps_video`, `fps_source`, `n_paired_frames` to stdout and to the markdown report. New `-T/--frame-tolerance INT` (default 2) errors loudly on count gaps beyond tolerance unless the user passes `-F/--frame-offset INT` (manual offset) or `-M/--truncate-mode {head,tail,intersection}`. Spearman correlation now computed at lags `-N..+N` frames (configurable via `-L/--lag-window`, default 5); the PNG and markdown report show lag-0 (primary), best-lag, and a per-lag bar chart. Best-lag != 0 indicates a clamp delay between edge approach and bounce response, which is informative even when lag-0 is weak. Reviewer's request 2026-05-05.
- **`tools/rank_zoom_variants.py` enforces explicit metric directionality.** Built-in directionality table for known assess_pixel_zoom metrics: `bounce_rate_per_s`, `zoom_velocity_log_p95`, `zoom_jerk_p95`, `zoom_cv`, `drift_per_minute` are all lower-is-better; `valid_frame_fraction` is higher-is-better. Passing `-D/--metric-direction` for a known metric errors (built-in cannot be overridden). Unknown metrics rejected unless `-D` is supplied. Legacy `-H/--higher-is-better` still works for unknown metrics but errors on known lower-is-better metrics. Reviewer's request 2026-05-05.
- **`docs/ZOOM_BOUNCE_REVIEW_RECIPE.md` updated.** Workflow gains Step 6 for `measure_black_bars.py`. Step 3 documents the new `--score` flag and the secondary `abs_smoothed` cross-check column. Step 4 documents the new lag plot, frame-alignment stanza, `--frame-offset`, and `--truncate-mode`. Step 2 documents the directionality table and `--metric-direction` flag. New "Decision logic" section codifies the reviewer's proceed-to-variant / choose-Path-A / choose-alpha-only / escalate criteria so DECISION.md has explicit branches.

### Additions and New Features

- **New tool `measure_seed_reproducibility.py`.** Reads only `tr_config/{basename}.track_runner.seeds.json` plus the co-located `interval_scores.json` for `race_start_frame`; no video file required. Filters pre-race visible seeds (frames in `[SEED_REPRODUCIBILITY_CORPUS.md](archive/SEED_REPRODUCIBILITY_CORPUS.md); analysis with interpretation in [SEED_REPRODUCIBILITY_FLOOR.md](archive/SEED_REPRODUCIBILITY_FLOOR.md). The previously-assumed "3 percent reproducibility floor" is too optimistic by an order of magnitude; the empirical fractional w stdev across well-sampled videos (n_pre >= 20) is 8 - 30 percent; fractional h stdev is 6 - 23 percent. Acceptance gates in the follow-up stabilizer plan must use relative reduction, not an absolute floor.

### Decisions and Failures

- **Torso-size stabilizer rejection CONFIRMED under revised zoom-bounce gate; M5 still BLOCKED.** Patch 5b of `~/.claude/plans/declarative-shimmying-brooks.md`. Reviewer feedback: the original gate `crop_h_fractional_velocity_p95` was height-only and used a first-order fractional approximation; the proper zoom-bounce gate matches `tools/assess_pixel_zoom.zoom_velocity_log_p95` but on the simulated final crop rectangle: `final_crop_log_velocity_p95 = p95(abs(diff(log(sqrt(final_crop_w * final_crop_h)))))`, plus a simulated hotspot severity (sum of top-5 window-local p95 peaks). Added `log_velocity` and `compute_simulated_hotspot_severity` helpers to `analyze_torso_box_noise.py`; new "Zoom-bounce gate (simulated final crop rectangle)" section in every per-video summary; new `final_crop_log_velocity` column in trace CSV. Re-ran the full 39-run grid against the revised metric. Maximum reduction across all 39 runs is **-3.4 percent** (median w=15 on IMG_3830) against a 30 percent Primary target. Hotspot severity drops on all 3 videos but by < 0.5 percent (technical Secondary pass, effectively flat). Conclusion stands: torso-h stabilization is decoupled from the simulated zoom-bounce signal because the crop pipeline's W+H averaging + two EMA passes + two Step 3.6 fit passes absorb the upstream variance. Library, flags, tests, and new metrics retained as diagnostic infrastructure. Updated comparison doc at [STABILIZER_METHOD_COMPARISON.md](archive/STABILIZER_METHOD_COMPARISON.md).

- **Torso-size stabilizer REJECTED for production integration; M5 (encode integration) BLOCKED.** Patch 5 / M4 of `~/.claude/plans/declarative-shimmying-brooks.md`. Ran the 39-run grid (`{median, hampel, mad_gated} x {5, 7, 9, 15}` plus baseline) on the 3 representative videos. The stabilizer reduces torso_h fractional p95 as designed (median w=15: -25.2 percent Glenbrook, -46.2 percent IMG_3823, -69.2 percent IMG_3830), but the reduction does NOT propagate to crop_h fractional p95 (worst case -3.8 percent on IMG_3830, +0.3 percent on Glenbrook). Both Primary acceptance gate (>= 50 percent torso_h reduction on >= 2 of 3 videos: only 1 of 3 hits) and Secondary gate (>= 30 percent crop_h reduction on >= 2 of 3 videos: 0 of 3 hit) FAIL. The architectural reading: torso_h noise is correlated with crop_h noise per the parent finding's rho +0.81 on Glenbrook, but the crop pipeline's W+H averaging, EMA, and Step 3.6 fit-to-source already absorb torso_h variance at a different magnitude scale. Reducing torso_h jitter does not move the crop_h velocity-p95 needle. Library and tests are kept as diagnostic infrastructure (`-S/-W` flags on `tools/analyze_torso_box_noise.py`, 4 new behavioral tests). M5 (encoder integration) and M5.A-C are not executed. Full evidence at [STABILIZER_METHOD_COMPARISON.md](archive/STABILIZER_METHOD_COMPARISON.md). Next mechanism to investigate (separate plan): the crop pipeline itself (Step 3.6 fit-to-source clamp interactions, W+H averaging, EMA staircase) per parent finding's deferred H2 and H3.

- **H1 (torso-box noise drives zoom bounce) SUPPORTED across the representative subset; fix space moves to torso-h stabilization in the per-frame observation step.** Closes Milestone 3 of `noisy-jittering-tendril.md`. New tool `analyze_torso_box_noise.py` re-runs the `tr_crop.direct_center_crop_trajectory` math against the on-disk solved trajectory and produces a per-frame `crop_zoom_trace.csv`, per-pass torso_h velocity_p95 (FWD / BWD / blended / final), per-hotspot 4-series PNGs (raw torso_h, median-filtered torso_h, smoothed crop_h, post-encode log_scale), and a per-video summary. Cross-video synthesis at [TORSO_NOISE_FINDING.md](archive/TORSO_NOISE_FINDING.md). Pearson rho between `abs(diff(torso_h))` and `abs(diff(crop_h_smoothed))` is +0.81 on Glenbrook (60fps), +0.27 on IMG_3830 (30fps), +0.19 on IMG_3823 (30fps); per-hotspot alignment is 14 of 15 (5/5 + 4/5 + 5/5) within +/- 60 frames of a torso-velocity p95+ spike. Per-pass torso_h velocity_p95 is identical at the FWD, BWD, blended, and final layers on all three videos (1.0, 7.0, 2.0 px respectively), so the noise enters BEFORE the blend in the per-frame observation step (blob snap, anchor fitting, or `velocity_model.raw_pred` rounding). The torso_h velocity_p95 values are exact integers, signature of integer-pixel rounding. Post-encode log_scale velocity is decoupled from torso_h velocity (rho ~ 0.0 on every video) -- the encoder + Fourier-Mellin assessment layer introduces independent noise that masks the upstream signal in the post-encode metric while local hotspot timing still aligns. H2 (Step 3.6 clamp amplification) and H3 (EMA staircase) deferred to follow-up; trace CSV contains the data for both. The crop pipeline (`tr_crop.py`), Step 3.6 clamp, and EMA default remain off-limits until torso-h stabilization is tested in isolation.

- **Step 3.6 fit-to-source ratchet hypothesis REJECTED; Path A CANCELLED; investigation pivots to torso-box trajectory noise.** Working hypothesis after Milestone 3a closed: **zoom bounce is caused by frame-to-frame noise in the solved torso box (especially h/w), and the crop algorithm faithfully tracks that noise into crop-size changes.** The encoded video is a downstream symptom; the cause is upstream of the crop pipeline. New plan `noisy-jittering-tendril.md` builds `tools/analyze_torso_box_noise.py` to emit a per-frame `crop_zoom_trace.csv` (torso h/w, derived crop sizes pre/post EMA, post-fit, edge-clamped flag, deltas, jerk) and a per-hotspot 4-series plot (raw torso_h, median-filtered torso_h, smoothed crop_h, encoded log_scale). Decision tree: (1) does torso-h velocity correlate with crop-h velocity above r=0.5? -> H1 supported; fix space moves to torso-h stabilization (median/Hampel filtering, outlier rejection, separated position-vs-size smoothing, blob-update downweighting). (2) does the clamped-frame crop_h_delta distribution show sign-asymmetry? -> Step 3.6 amplifies as secondary effect. (3) does pre-vs-post EMA show staircase? -> note for follow-up. The earlier video-frequency follow-ups (`meandering-drifting-tide.md`, `staccato-flickering-pixel.md`) were superseded for targeting the wrong layer; both have SUPERSEDED banners pointing at `noisy-jittering-tendril.md`.

- **Step 3.6 fit-to-source ratchet hypothesis REJECTED by baseline diagnostic; Path A CANCELLED.** Milestone 3a baseline-only diagnostic (10 of 11 baseline `*_tracked.mkv` files in `TRACK_VIDEOS/`; Lyra-Wheeling-IMG_3912 excluded due to invalid mediainfo fps) ran `find_zoom_hotspots.py` on all videos and `correlate_bounce_with_edge.py` + `spectrum_zoom_bounce.py` on the top-3 worst-bouncing subset (Glenbrook-1600m, IMG_3830, IMG_3823). All three correlators returned essentially zero or weakly negative Spearman rho between bounce and edge proximity (lag-0: -0.102, -0.020, -0.014; best-lag scan over `-5..+5` did not rescue the hypothesis). The reviewer's proceed-to-Path-A criterion ("edge correlation positive in >= 2 of 3 representative videos") is NOT met (0 of 3). Path A (`crop_centered_fit_to_source: False`) would not fix the residual bounce; **Milestone 2b (Path A variant production) is cancelled and Milestone 3b (variant comparison) is blocked**. Spectrum analysis surfaced two distinct regimes that neither Path A nor alpha tuning would target: 60fps races concentrate energy below the EMA cutoff (~0.024 Hz dominant on Glenbrook = 42-second drift period), and 30fps races concentrate energy near Nyquist (~14 Hz on IMG_3823 / IMG_3830 = single-frame jitter consistent with integer-pixel rounding or compression artifact). Investigation split into two focused follow-up plans, **mechanism-first, no tuning until a specific measurable mechanism is named**: (1) `meandering-drifting-tide.md` targets the 60fps low-frequency drift (Glenbrook primary; tests camera-motion cumulative drift, crop-controller lag, scene-transform integration drift, torso-box size walk); (2) `staccato-flickering-pixel.md` targets the 30fps near-Nyquist jitter (IMG_3830 + IMG_3823 primary; tests integer-pixel crop rounding, bbox center rounding, encoder rate-control flutter, source-content single-frame motion). Full evidence package in [BASELINE_DIAGNOSTIC.md](archive/BASELINE_DIAGNOSTIC.md); cross-corpus index in `HOTSPOTS_INDEX.md`. Original plan archived to [TRACK_RUNNER_ZOOM_BOUNCE_INVESTIGATION_2026-05.md](archive/TRACK_RUNNER_ZOOM_BOUNCE_INVESTIGATION_2026-05.md).

### Developer Tests and Notes

- **`tests/test_tr_zoom_bounce_tools.py` extended to 22 tests.** Two new behavioral tests for the V1 rolling-min source-fit ceiling helper: (1) `smooth_ceiling_w <= geometric_ceiling_w` and `S5_v1_w <= max(smooth_ceiling_w, 1.0)` per frame on a sawtooth-cx fixture (boundary safety); (2) on the same fixture, V1's S5 log_velocity_p95 is strictly less than production's S5 log_velocity_p95 (mechanism behavior). Tests landed proactively under Patch 3 because they exercise the helper that already exists; they remain valid whether or not Patch 4 promotes V1 to production. All 22 tests pass on a synthetic-fixture-only run with no video I/O.

- **`tests/test_tr_zoom_bounce_tools.py` extended to 20 tests.** Two new behavioral tests for the per-stage bisect helpers in `tools/analyze_torso_box_noise.py`: (1) shape coverage -- `compute_pre_encode_crop_signals` returns all six stage keys `S0_raw, S1_w_h_avg, S2_ema_1, S3_fit_1, S4_ema_2, S5_fit_2`, each with `h` and `w` float64 arrays of length n_frames, plus the legacy keys; (2) stationary-trajectory zero-velocity sanity -- a 200-frame constant trajectory produces `log_velocity_p95 <= 1e-12` and `hotspot_severity <= 1e-12` on every stage. Tests use synthetic numpy fixtures only; no video I/O. Patch 3 of `~/.claude/plans/lucky-napping-lake.md` (WP-B1, WP-B2).

- **`tests/test_tr_zoom_bounce_tools.py` extended to 18 tests.** Four new behavioral tests for `track_runner/torso_size_stabilizer.py`: (1) median suppresses a one-frame outlier and preserves quiet frames within 1 px; (2) Hampel pulls the outlier toward the local median while leaving distant frames byte-equal to input; (3) mad_gated also pulls the outlier toward the local median on the constant + outlier fixture; (4) cx/cy arrays returned byte-identical for every method/window per C5 size-vs-position separation. Test fixtures are pure numpy with no video I/O. Patch 3 of `~/.claude/plans/declarative-shimmying-brooks.md` (M2.B stabilizer tests).

- **`tools/analyze_torso_box_noise.py` wires stabilizer for method/window comparison.** New `-S/--stabilizer-method` (choices `none, median, hampel, mad_gated`; default `none`) and `-W/--stabilizer-window` (int; default 7) flags route the loaded trajectory through `track_runner.torso_size_stabilizer.stabilize_trajectory` before the crop-signal computation. The pre-existing `-W/--median-window` was renamed to `--reference-window` (long-only, no short flag) to free `-W` for the stabilizer; the renamed flag still controls the diagnostic median-filtered torso_h reference series shown on the hotspot plot. With `-S none`, the trace CSV is byte-equal to the M1 baseline (verified on IMG_3823, MD5 16640f8). Per-method runs land at `EVIDENCE/torso_noise/{basename}/{method}_w{window}_torso_noise_summary.md` so the M4 grid does not overwrite. Patch 4 of `~/.claude/plans/declarative-shimmying-brooks.md` (M3.A).

- **`tools/analyze_torso_box_noise.py` emits fractional velocity metrics.** New helper `velocity_fractional` returns `abs(diff(values)) / abs(values[1:])` with NaN guards for non-finite or near-zero divisors. Trace CSV gains 3 columns: `torso_h_fractional_velocity`, `torso_w_fractional_velocity`, `crop_h_fractional_velocity`. Per-video summary gains a "Fractional jitter (p95)" table and a fractional-units row in the Pearson correlation table. Pixel-unit columns kept (intra-video meaningful per contract C4); fractional columns are cross-video comparable per C2. Baseline measured on 3 representative videos at `FRACTIONAL_BASELINE.md`: Glenbrook 0.91 / 1.15 / 6.28 percent (torso_h / torso_w / crop_h), IMG_3823 7.14 / 9.09 / 2.64 percent, IMG_3830 13.51 / 20.00 / 1.76 percent. Patch 1 of `~/.claude/plans/declarative-shimmying-brooks.md` (M1 fractional metrics + baseline).

Copied [TORSO_NOISE_FINDING.md](archive/TORSO_NOISE_FINDING.md) to [TORSO_NOISE_FINDING.md](archive/TORSO_NOISE_FINDING.md), and [BASELINE_DIAGNOSTIC.md](archive/BASELINE_DIAGNOSTIC.md) to [BASELINE_DIAGNOSTIC.md](archive/BASELINE_DIAGNOSTIC.md). - **`tests/test_tr_zoom_bounce_tools.py` extended to 14 tests.** Two new behavioral tests for `tools/analyze_torso_box_noise.py` (per noisy-jittering-tendril.md WP 1.2): (1) `velocity_abs` of a constructed cosine peaks at the zero-crossing indices (steepest-slope invariant) within +/- 10 percent of one quarter-period, (2) `median_filter_1d` with window 7 suppresses a single one-frame outlier by more than an order of magnitude while `velocity_abs` retains the spike at the outlier index. Both tests are pure-numpy, no video I/O.

- **`tests/test_tr_zoom_bounce_tools.py` created (12 tests).** Behavioral invariants only, per docs/PYTHON_STYLE.md "good tests" rules: no asserts on hardcoded constants, exact metric values, or collection sizes. Tests cover: hotspot descending order, hotspot non-overlap, velocity_p95 distinguishes drift from jitter, correlation positive on constructed signal, lagged Spearman recovers a +2-frame constructed lag, frame-alignment raises on mismatch, FFT recovers a constructed sinusoid frequency within +/-10%, ranking ordering with deltas, lower-vs-higher direction flips verdict, known-metric direction blocks override, black-bar height matches constructed top bar, black-bar area matches inclusion-exclusion analytic. The test module had been claimed as shipped under Patch 5 in a previous session summary but the file was missing from the repo; created from scratch. Adds `tools/` and repo root to sys.path locally (NOT via conftest.py per project memory).

## 2026-05-04

### Additions and New Features

- **`--auto-bin [HEIGHT]` flag added to `solve` and `refine`.** Computes `bin_factor = max(1, round(source_height / target))` from the probed source height (via [probe_video.py](../common_tools/probe_video.py)), so a single batch script can target a binned vertical resolution across mixed-resolution inputs without picking a per-video `--bin`. Bare `--auto-bin` defaults to target 480; pass `--auto-bin 720` for 720. `bin_factor` is a whole number, so the actual binned height only approximates the target (e.g. 1080->bin2->540, 2160->bin4->540, 2816->bin6->469, 1540->bin3->513 then goodbox-snapped to 480). At target=720: 1080->bin1 (1080), 1440->bin2 (720), 2160->bin3 (720). Round (not floor) was chosen so `--auto-bin 720` on a 1080-line source actually does what the user typed something larger asks for; concretely floor breaks "--auto-bin 720" on 1080 sources (gives bin=1, no binning). Wired into a mutually exclusive group with `--bin` in [cli_args.py](../track_runner/cli_args.py) `_add_bin_arg`; resolved against `video_info["height"]` in [cli.py](../track_runner/cli.py) `_run_solver` immediately before the existing `bin_factor >= 1` validation, with a one-line log showing source height, chosen bin, and resulting actual height. [re-solve.sh](../re-solve.sh) updated: both the first-pass `solve --yes` and the second-pass `solve --upgrade` invocations append `--auto-bin` so re-solving a mixed batch (1080p + 4K + 720p sources) hits roughly 540-line working frames without per-video flags.

- **`FrameReader` no longer emits "source_width/source_height is not divisible by bin_factor" warnings.** Non-divisible source dims silently drop at most `(bin_factor - 1)` right/bottom pixels, which is identical in spirit to the goodbox right/bottom crop already applied a few lines later in `_resolve_frame_geometry`. The loss is bounded by `bin-1` over thousands of pixels (e.g. 2 of 2816 columns at bin=3 = 0.07%), far under the goodbox `_MAX_CROP_FRACTION` 10% safety floor that already gates a real warning when a snap is too aggressive. The previous warning fired once per `FrameReader` instance and was being emitted dozens of times per solve when `--auto-bin` picks an odd bin against a typical 16:9 source ([frame_reader.py](../common_tools/frame_reader.py) `_resolve_frame_geometry`). Wired into a mutually exclusive group with `--bin` in [cli_args.py](../track_runner/cli_args.py) `_add_bin_arg`; resolved against `video_info["height"]` in [cli.py](../track_runner/cli.py) `_run_solver` immediately before the existing `bin_factor >= 1` validation, with a one-line log showing source height, chosen bin, and resulting actual height. [re-solve.sh](../re-solve.sh) updated: both the first-pass `solve --yes` and the second-pass `solve --upgrade` invocations append `--auto-bin` so re-solving a mixed batch (1080p + 4K + 720p sources) hits roughly 540-line working frames without per-video flags.

### Behavior or Interface Changes

- **`cli.main()` rejects non-`.mkv` input early with a clear remux instruction.** The `.mkv`-only restriction was previously enforced inside `FrameReader` (see [frame_reader.py](../common_tools/frame_reader.py) line 228), but `cli.main()` ran `_probe_video` and three identity-mismatch checks first, so users who passed a `.mov` saw probe output and then a confusing trace from a deeper layer. The guard now fires immediately after the input-file existence check at [cli.py](../track_runner/cli.py) `main()`, with a one-line message that names the reason (random-access seek is too slow on `.mov`/`.mp4`) and prints a ready-to-run `mkvmerge -o <stem>.mkv <input>` command. The lower-layer FrameReader guard is left in place as a safety net for the few `tools/` scripts that bypass `cli`.
- **`tr_config/` data filenames now key off the source-video stem, not the full basename.** Previously [tr_paths.py](../track_runner/tr_paths.py) `_data_file_path` joined `os.path.basename(input_file) + suffix`, so `foo.mov` produced `tr_config/foo.mov.track_runner.seeds.json`. Remuxing the source from `.mov` to `.mkv` (now mandatory; see prior bullet) silently orphaned every artifact. The helper now strips the source extension via `os.path.splitext`, so the same source produces `tr_config/foo.track_runner.seeds.json` regardless of container. All seven `default_*` helpers (`config`, `seeds`, `diagnostics`, `intervals`, `torso_box_coords`, `encode_analysis`, `camera_motion`) inherit the new naming through the shared helper; `default_output_path` and `default_race_start_contact_sheet_path` were already stem-based and are unchanged. **Migration for existing `tr_config/` directories:** run once per repo, `cd tr_config && for f in *.mov.* *.MOV.* *.mp4.* *.MP4.*; do [ -f "$f" ] || continue; mv "$f" "$(echo "$f" | sed -E 's/\.(mov|MOV|mp4|MP4)\././')"; done`. 67 focused tests across `test_tr_encode_mode.py`, `test_tr_state_io.py`, and `tr_paths`-related suites pass unchanged.
- **`_check_identity_mismatch` no longer aborts solve on stale-schema artifacts.** The function's docstring promises "warn only, never reject on identity mismatch" but the `.npz` branch called `state_io.load_torso_box_coords`, which raises a hard `RuntimeError` for any `torso_box_coords` schema below the active supported set. A user trying to re-solve a video whose previous run produced a v8 npz hit the schema rejection during the pre-dispatch identity check and was told to "Please re-solve the video" -- which is what they were running. The npz branch in [cli.py](../track_runner/cli.py) `_check_identity_mismatch` now wraps the load in a narrow try/except, prints a single warning line that the file is unreadable and will be overwritten, and returns. Solve then proceeds and rewrites the file at the current schema. No change to `state_io.load_torso_box_coords` itself; the schema gate remains the source of truth for downstream consumers that actually need the coords.

- **Stage 3 per-interval console lines drop the stale `blob_accept` column; Stage 4 now emits one structured result line per interval replacing four chatter prints.** Stage 3 runs Hermite-only so `blob_accept` was always `n/a/n/a`; the column is removed and a trailing `[stage3]` tag added so solve logs are scannable. Stage 4 replaces `blob start:` / `blob done:` / `[blob] dispatch` / `[blob] complete` with a single line per completed interval that mirrors the Stage 3 column layout, shows real `blob_accept=FF%/BB%` coverage, appends `(+/-X.XX)` deltas vs the Stage 3 baseline for each metric, and ends with `[stage4]`. When no Stage 3 baseline exists (e.g. a seeded refine that bypassed Stage 3), deltas are omitted and the line still emits cleanly. Touched files: [solve_queue.py](../track_runner/solve_queue.py), [interval_solver.py](../track_runner/interval_solver.py). Reference: plan `~/.claude/plans/floating-launching-wozniak.md`.

  ```
  Before (Stage 3):
    interval 16380-16800 (7.0s)  overlap=0.01  vel_smooth=1.00  size_smooth=0.35  blob_accept=n/a/n/a  [WEAK: overlap]
  Before (Stage 4, four separate lines):
    blob start: pair_idx=5 frames 16380-16800
    [blob] dispatch pair_idx=5 frames 16380-16800
    [blob] complete pair_idx=5 elapsed=3.2s
    blob done:  pair_idx=5 elapsed=3.2s

  After (Stage 3):
    interval 16380-16800 (7.0s)  overlap=0.01  vel_smooth=1.00  size_smooth=0.35  [WEAK: overlap] [stage3]
  After (Stage 4, one line):
    interval 16380-16800 (7.0s)  overlap=0.42 (+0.41)  vel_smooth=1.00 (+0.00)  size_smooth=0.71 (+0.36)  blob_accept=83%/77%  [GOOD] [stage4]
  ```

- **`FrameReader` rolled back from PyAV to `cv2.VideoCapture`.** PyAV's bundled libav family (`libavdevice.62.x`) collided in-process with OpenCV's bundled libav (`libavdevice.61.x`), producing the macOS Objective-C duplicate-class warning at every import (`objc[..]: Class AVFFrameReceiver is implemented in both .../cv2/.dylibs/libavdevice.61.3.100.dylib and .../av/.dylibs/libavdevice.62.1.100.dylib`). Decode and encode now share a single libav family. Public API of `FrameReader` is unchanged: `read_frame`, `seek_for_encode`, `__iter__`, `__enter__`, `__exit__`, `close`, plus the `video_path/frame_count/fps/width/height/geometry/bin_factor` properties all behave identically. Internally only two seek strategies remain: strategy 0 (sequential fast-path; no `cap.set` when `frame_index == self._cap_next_index`) and strategy 1 (`cap.set(CAP_PROP_POS_FRAMES, idx)` random access). The 5-strategy waterfall and mkvmerge remux fallback from the pre-PyAV era are not restored; instead `FrameReader.__init__` rejects non-`.mkv` paths with a `ValueError` containing the exact `mkvmerge -o out.mkv in.mov` command. The Stage-4 sequential pre-pass in [residual_pre_pass.py](../track_runner/residual_pre_pass.py) was unchanged and continues to absorb scattered random access into bounded per-interval sequential walks (40-frame rolling cap), so the cv2 strategy-1 cost is not on the hot path. Reference: plan `~/.claude/plans/iterative-tickling-dongarra.md`.
- **Source video format hard-restricted to `.mkv`.** MP4/MOV users must remux losslessly via `mkvmerge -o input.mkv input.mov` once before use. The pipeline does not transcode. [INSTALL.md](INSTALL.md) updated; [README.md](../common_tools/README.md) rewritten to describe the cv2-only reader and document the `.mkv` requirement.

### Removals and Deprecations

- **`av` (PyAV) dropped from `pip_requirements.txt`.** Local installs may keep `av` for a while; the manifest no longer requires it. Users may run `python3 -m pip uninstall av` to free the duplicate libav dylibs from the environment. The PyAV-specific test files referenced in the prior 2026-05-04 entry (`test_pyav_decode_backend.py`, `test_pyav_smoke.py`, `test_pyav_residual_consistency.py`, `test_frame_reader_protocol.py`) had already been cleaned up before the rollback; one new test file `test_frame_reader.py` replaces them with two focused tests (smoke read + non-mkv rejection).

### Fixes and Maintenance

- **Synthetic test fixtures switched from `.mp4` (mp4v fourcc) to `.mkv` (FFV1 fourcc).** `test_tr_frame_reader_bin.py` and [test_tr_camera_motion_bin.py](../tests/test_tr_camera_motion_bin.py) write their throwaway test videos as MKV so they pass the new `.mkv` init guard; FFV1 in MKV is lossless and supported by the cv2 FFmpeg backend. Both files now use `cv2.VideoWriter_fourcc(*"FFV1")` and `tmp_path / "v.mkv"` (or `trans.mkv`).

### Additions and New Features

- **`interval_solver.collect_erased_frames(seeds, fps, total_frames)`** new public helper that returns the set of frame indices `_apply_trajectory_erasure` would wipe for a given seed list, mirroring the per-seed `not_in_frame` radius logic without mutating any trajectory. analyze --plot uses it to emit a `frames_erased: [bool]` array in the HTML report JSON so the renderer can distinguish user-flagged dropouts from generic tracker gaps (both still serialize as null in the panel series). Reference: plan `~/.claude/plans/rustling-juggling-creek.md` P1-1.
- **PyAV decode backend now the default and exclusive decode path in `common_tools/frame_reader.py`.** HEVC-in-MOV reads natively without remux. Reference: plan `~/.claude/plans/golden-plotting-tulip.md` M2/M4.

- **`FrameReader` is now PyAV-only.** The `backend=` kwarg is removed; cv2-based decode (5-strategy seek dance, mkvmerge remux fallback) deleted. Reference: plan M5.WS1.
- **`--debug-blob` CLI flag and its instrumentation removed.** The flag instrumented the cv2 5-strategy decode dance, which no longer exists. Reference: plan M5.WS1.

Reference: plan M5.WS3.

Reference: plan M5.WS1.

### Fixes and Maintenance

- **Code-review cleanup pass on past-week's changes.** (1) Removed bare `except Exception: pass` wrapping the Stage-4 blob-pool drain loop in [interval_solver.py](../track_runner/interval_solver.py) (~line 1529-1563); worker `fut.result()` exceptions now propagate as intended instead of being silently swallowed mid-stage, per [PYTHON_STYLE.md](PYTHON_STYLE.md) "DO NOT HIDE BUGS WITH DEFAULTS" and "use try/except rarely". (2) Replaced `entry_lo.get("conf", 0.0) or 0.0` with direct `entry_lo["conf"]` indexing at `track_runner/residual_motion.py:808-809`; the double-default coerced legitimate `0.0` and hid missing keys, while `"conf"` is a structurally required trajectory-entry key (see `state_io.py:96`). (3) Dropped migration-debris `getattr(reader, "width", 1920)` / `"height", 1080)` / `"geometry", None)` defaults in [residual_pre_pass.py](../track_runner/residual_pre_pass.py) lines 162-166; with the PyAV-only `FrameReader`, all three attrs are concrete `@property` definitions, so the defaults could only paper over a broken reader by silently producing wrong-resolution ROIs. (4) Unquoted `"dict | None"` -> `dict | None` return annotation at `residual_pre_pass.py:84` (Python 3.12 supports the syntax natively). Pushed back on two reviewer findings: the `.get(default)` clusters in `state_io.py:861-878` and `solve_queue.py:266-294` are intentional v2/v3 dual-schema legacy paths (the surrounding comments explicitly call out "preserve any present numeric fields" and "v2 legacy format"), not live read paths, so defensive defaults are correct there. Tests: 18 focused tests green (`test_tr_residual_pre_pass.py`, `test_tr_residual_motion_bin.py`, `test_tr_solver_driver.py`); pyflakes clean on all three touched files.

### Developer Tests and Notes

- **`track_runner/ui/seed_controller.py` now imports no `cv2`; `track_runner/tr_detection.py` normalizes OpenCV failures to `RuntimeError`.** The seed UI only needed `cv2` to name an exception type, so removing that import keeps the controller cv2-free without changing detector fallback behavior.

- **Final M5 verification: `pytest tests/` key suites pass after cleanup.** `test_tr_camera_motion.py` (15 tests), `test_tr_solver_driver.py` (5 tests), `test_pyflakes_code_lint.py` (131 tests) all green. Full suite run deferred pending tool migration completion. Reference: plan M5 verification checklist.

### Additions and New Features (Earlier)

- **M1: new `common_tools/probe_video.py` exposing `probe_video(path) -> dict`.** Backed by mediainfo CLI. Returns dict with keys width/height/fps/frame_count/duration_s. Replaces five inline cv2.VideoCapture probe blocks across [seeding.py](../track_runner/seeding.py) (x2), [seed_editor.py](../track_runner/seed_editor.py), and (via `_encode_segment` and `encode_cropped_video_parallel`) [encoder.py](../track_runner/encoder.py). Reference: plan `~/.claude/plans/golden-plotting-tulip.md` M1.WS1.

- **M1: new `FrameReader.seek_for_encode(start_frame)` method.** Seeks the cv2 capture and arms the sequential fast-path so the next `read_frame(start_frame)` hits strategy 0 with no second cap.set. Encoder driver replaces its `reader.cap.set(POS_FRAMES, ...)` reach-in with this public method. Reference: plan M1.WS2.

- **M1: new `FrameReader.__iter__`, `__enter__`, `__exit__` ported from VideoReader.** Iteration resets to frame 0, arms the fast-path, advances the tracker per yield, and invalidates on EOF. Context manager calls `close()` on exit. Reference: plan M1.WS3 (audit at WP-M1.5).

### Behavior or Interface Changes

- **Goodbox snap now applies at any `bin_factor` including 1 in `common_tools/frame_reader.py`.** Previously bin_factor=1 short-circuited and returned source dims unchanged; now Stage 1 phase correlate and Stage 4 residual algorithms always see FFT-friendly dims regardless of bin. On non-goodbox sources (1080-row HD: 1080->1056, 24px crop bottom; 2160-row 4K: 2160->2112, 48px crop bottom). Goodbox sources (1920-col, 320x240, etc.) are unchanged. Existing trajectories were solved at the previous (uncropped) dims and need re-solving via `re-solve.sh` after the M3 smoke milestone lands. Reference: plan side-quest in M1; original ask from the user during planning of `~/.claude/plans/golden-plotting-tulip.md`.

- **`track_runner/encoder.py` and `track_runner/cli.py` no longer import `video_io.VideoReader` for production decode.** Both use [frame_reader.py](../common_tools/frame_reader.py) `FrameReader` with [probe_video.py](../common_tools/probe_video.py) `probe_video` for metadata. `track_runner/video_io.py` itself is unchanged; it stays in the repo because (a) `VideoWriter` is still imported by `encoder.py`, and (b) `VideoReader` remains used by 5 `tools/` scripts whose migration is deferred to M5. Reference: plan M1.WS3 (combined WP-M1.4 + WP-M1.6).

- **`track_runner/seeding.py` no longer imports `cv2` at all (probes migrated to `probe_video`); `track_runner/seed_editor.py` retains `cv2` for overlay drawing only.** Reference: plan M1.WS1 (WP-M1.2).

- **`docs/CHANGELOG.md` rotated to keep file size under the 1000-line threshold.** Per [REPO_STYLE.md](REPO_STYLE.md) the changelog rotates when it reaches about 1000 lines. The previous active file was at 1082 lines spanning 2026-03-24 through 2026-05-04. Day blocks for 2026-05-02 and earlier moved to [CHANGELOG-2026-05a.md](CHANGELOG-2026-05a.md); the active file now holds only the last two date headings (2026-05-04 and 2026-05-03) and ends with an "Older entries" link to the archive. Reverse-chronological order preserved in both files; no day block was split across files.

### Fixes and Maintenance

- **`track_runner/encoder.py` sequential `encode_cropped_video` now wraps the frame loop in `try/finally` around `writer.close()`.** An exception inside the loop previously skipped the close call and left the ffmpeg subprocess with an unflushed stdin pipe; the parallel-segment workers already paired close in `try/finally`. Reference: plan `~/.claude/plans/rustling-juggling-creek.md` P2-2.

- **`track_runner/tr_crop.py::trajectory_to_crop_rects` asserts `len(trajectory) <= total_frames` and documents the gap-fill policy.** A trajectory longer than the source frame count is always a wiring bug (mismatched `video_info`, stale solve cache); failing loudly prevents the same class of mismatch that crashed analyze on 2026-05-04 from recurring silently. Shorter input keeps the existing hold-last-known padding behavior. Two new tests in [test_tr_crop_assertion.py](../tests/test_tr_crop_assertion.py). Reference: plan `~/.claude/plans/rustling-juggling-creek.md` P2-1.

- **`track_runner/cli.py` now lazily imports both `encoder` and `common_tools.frame_filters` inside encode-only code paths.** Non-encode subcommands no longer load the OpenCV-heavy encode stack at CLI startup, and the in-code comments explain why those imports must stay lazy so future cleanup does not reintroduce the duplicate FFmpeg warning.

- **`track_runner/cli.py` now imports `encoder` lazily inside `_mode_encode`.** Non-encode subcommands no longer load the OpenCV-heavy encoder module at CLI startup, which keeps the solve/refine path from importing OpenCV through the encode stack unless encode mode is actually selected.

- **`common_tools/frame_reader.py` no longer imports `cv2`; PyAV now handles the reader resize path.** The decode path stays PyAV-only, so importing `FrameReader` no longer loads OpenCV's bundled FFmpeg libraries into the same process. This reduces the chance of the macOS Objective-C duplicate-class warning when solve/refine code uses PyAV decode alongside OpenCV-heavy tools. The `bin_factor` and goodbox behavior are unchanged from the caller's perspective.

- **Audit-driven cleanup of `analyze --plot` patches.** Four parallel review agents (plan completeness, Python style, pytest strict, markdown docs) audited the 9-patch `analyze --plot` work and surfaced a small punch list of must-fix and should-fix items. (1) [cli.py](../track_runner/cli.py) `_mode_analyze` try/except body collapsed to one line per the [PYTHON_STYLE.md](PYTHON_STYLE.md) "two-line max" rule; the stderr-print moved out of the except block and now runs whenever `motion_track is None`. (2) [analyze_report.py](../track_runner/analyze_report.py) `_build_analyze_report_data` removed the `if trajectory is not None else 0` defensive default per the "do not hide bugs with defaults" rule; callers always pass a list (possibly empty), never `None`. (3) Type hints added to seven public/private functions in `analyze_report.py`: `write_analyze_report`, `_build_analyze_report_data`, `_write_analyze_report_html`, `_zoom_stability_panel_data`, `_zoom_multiple_panel_data`, `_camera_motion_panel_data`, `_runner_speed_panel_data` (return type already present, parameters now typed). (4) [test_tr_analyze_report.py](../tests/test_tr_analyze_report.py) deduplicated `_make_synthetic_inputs` and `_IdentitySceneTransform` (previously defined separately in the test file and in [analyze_report_fixtures.py](../tests/analyze_report_fixtures.py)); test file now imports both from the fixtures module so a future schema change updates one place. (5) Six fragile test assertions relaxed: three panel-shape tests dropped exact series-name list checks (they baked in window-size constants like `crop_h_9f_mean`); two "len(panels) == 4" checks relaxed to `>= 4` and `panel_ids[:4] == required` to allow future panel additions; `pytest.raises(AssertionError, match='trajectory length')` dropped its `match=` to decouple from error-message wording; the `'configured' in ref['label']` check dropped to avoid locking in label text. Behavioral coverage preserved (axis assignments still asserted by counting left/right axes; reference-line value still asserted; series counts still asserted). 38/38 tests pass.

- **Docs updated for `analyze --plot` artifacts.** [ANALYZE.md](modes/ANALYZE.md) "Output files" section now lists the HTML report at `tr_config/<stem>.encode_analysis.html`. [CODE_ARCHITECTURE.md](CODE_ARCHITECTURE.md) "Crop, encode, and analysis" module map adds `track_runner/analyze_report.py`. [FILE_STRUCTURE.md](FILE_STRUCTURE.md) tree adds `analyze_report.py`, the new top-level `data/js/analyze_report_renderer.js` asset, the test file, and the fixture module.

- **M6: bounded rolling buffer in `track_runner/residual_pre_pass.py` (post-OOM regression hotfix).** The 2026-05-03 M3+M4 ship held the entire interval+padding range of frames in a single dict with no eviction (`frame_cache = {}` followed by populating both `("bgr", fi)` and `fi` keys for every frame in `[pad_lo, pad_hi]`). At `--bin 2` across 7 workers this multiplied to ~40 GB; at `--bin 1` ~157 GB. The user's MacStudio (36 GB) crashed. Same shape of bug as the 2026-04-25 OOM; the M3 implementer documented `MAX_GRAY_CACHE_FRAMES = 40` in `residual_motion.py` and then sidestepped the safety principle in the new module. Fix in [residual_pre_pass.py](../track_runner/residual_pre_pass.py): replace the unbounded walk-then-compute with an interleaved walk-drain-evict that holds at most `2 * half_window * stride + 1` frames in each of two split buffers (`bgr_buf: collections.OrderedDict[int, BGR-uint8]`, `gray_buf: dict[int, gray-float32]`). Eviction is by numeric frame index on each buffer independently, eliminating the mixed-key foot-guns of a single dict (`len(buf)` ambiguity, insertion-order interleaving stopping eviction early at a newer gray-int key while older BGR-tuple keys leak). New module-level constant `MAX_PREPASS_BUFFER_FRAMES = 40` matches `residual_motion.MAX_GRAY_CACHE_FRAMES = 40` so a single mental model covers both modules; raises `RuntimeError` (not `assert`) when either buffer exceeds the cap, surfacing algorithmic regressions as a clear memory-limit error rather than a system OOM. Per-worker peak ~145 MB at `--bin 4`; total pool budget ~1.0 GB; `max_tasks_per_child=1` continues as the mechanical failsafe. Compute uses a small throwaway adapter dict (~9 entries) populated per center from the rolling buffers, so `compute_residual_for_frame` never falls back to scattered `reader.read_frame` calls. Output is byte-identical to the on-the-fly reader path (`numpy.array_equal` on uint8 residuals + validity for every `(frame_index, roi)` key). New optional parameters `debug_stats: dict | None = None` and `read_log: list | None = None` on `precompute_interval_residuals` for test instrumentation; production callers leave both None and the signature is unchanged at the existing call site. No schema bump (output is byte-identical). No public API change. Tests: six new in `test_tr_residual_pre_pass.py` (algorithmic bound on short and long intervals, safety-net cap fires, padding stays BGR-only via `gray_frames` set, no-fallback-reader-reads via `read_log`, output parity vs sequential reader baseline); existing four M3 tests pass unchanged. Total tests in the file: 10. Stage 4 parity tests pass (53 across `test_tr_residual_motion_bin.py`, `test_tr_residual_motion_window.py`, `test_tr_residual_heat_map.py`, `test_tr_blob_snap.py`, `test_tr_velocity_model.py`, `test_tr_stage4_parity.py`). Plan: `~/.claude/plans/memoized-percolating-moler.md` (M6).

- **`analyze --plot` no longer crashes with `AssertionError: trajectory length N != crop_rects length M` on real videos.** [cli.py](../track_runner/cli.py) `_mode_analyze` now pads `trajectory` with `None` entries to match `video_info["frame_count"]` before calling `analyze_report.write_analyze_report`. The mismatch arises because [tr_crop.py](../track_runner/tr_crop.py) `trajectory_to_crop_rects` always pads its output to the full source frame count via gap-filling, whereas the upstream `trajectory` is the post-erasure, post-anchored list which can be hundreds of frames shorter (e.g. the live-run `Lyra-Wheeling-IMG_3912.mkv` had 27145-frame trajectory vs 27348-frame crop_rects, a 203-frame gap from `_apply_trajectory_erasure`). Padding with `None` is semantically correct: those frames have no torso geometry, so the panel builders emit `null` in the affected positions and the renderer draws gaps. Two new tests in [test_tr_analyze_report.py](../tests/test_tr_analyze_report.py) lock in the contract: `test_zoom_panels_require_aligned_trajectory_and_crop_rects` documents that the panel-data builders demand equal-length inputs, and `test_integration_trajectory_padded_to_match_crop_rects` exercises the cli.py wiring path with a deliberately-shorter trajectory padded with trailing `None`s, asserting the JSON `torso_h` series ends in five `null` entries while early entries remain populated. Total tests in the file: 38.
