Bin tests in `test_tr_frame_reader_bin.py` extended with three new cases for goodbox-at-bin-1 (1080->1056, 2160->2112, no-snap-when-goodbox). Total new tests: 14. An earlier draft added 32 tests (including static source-regex fragility and trivial property checks); trimmed per [PYTHON_STYLE.md](PYTHON_STYLE.md) "What makes a good test" / "will this test still pass next week without code changes." Plan: `~/.claude/plans/golden-plotting-tulip.md`.

## 2026-05-03

### Developer Tests and Notes

- **`tests/analyze_report_fixtures.py` and three integration tests in `tests/test_tr_analyze_report.py` Group G (Patch 9 of analyze-mode HTML diagnostic report plan).** New shared fixture module exports `make_synthetic_inputs()` which returns a deterministic 30-frame dataset (trajectory + matching crop rects + MotionTrack-shaped object + identity SceneTransform stub + fps + config). Three Group G integration tests call `write_analyze_report` directly (no subprocess) under the three CLI degradation paths -- full inputs (4 panels), motion missing (2 panels + warning), scene transform missing (3 panels + warning) -- and assert HTML structure + JSON schema for each. Total test count for `tests/test_tr_analyze_report.py`: 36.

### Behavior or Interface Changes

- **M2: fps-invariant 9-sample neighbor window with linear stride (plan `~/.claude/plans/memoized-percolating-moler.md` M2).**
  `DEFAULT_BACKGROUND_WINDOW_SECONDS` and `resolve_half_window()` removed from
  [residual_motion.py](../track_runner/residual_motion.py). Replaced with
  `REFERENCE_FPS = 60` and `resolve_stride(fps)` which returns
  `stride = max(1, round(fps / REFERENCE_FPS))`. Neighbor offsets in
  `compute_residual_for_frame` are now `k * stride` (k != 0). At 60 fps stride=1,
  offsets `[-4..-1, 1..4]` -- byte-identical to today's behavior. At 119.94 fps
  stride=2, offsets `[-8, -6, -4, -2, 2, 4, 6, 8]` -- same ~133 ms time span, half
  the I/O vs the old 17-neighbor window. At 240 fps stride=4, quarter the I/O.
  `precompute_interval_residuals` gains a `stride` parameter; padding is
  `half_window * stride` so the BGR cache covers the wider time-span window at
  high fps. `observe_blob_at`, `compute_heat_map_roi`, and the diagnose tool all
  migrated to the stride model. SCHEMA_VERSION bumped to 11; v10 torso_box_coords
  files remain readable (`SUPPORTED_ARTIFACT_SCHEMAS["torso_box_coords"] = {10, 11}`).

### Additions and New Features

- **M3+M4: per-worker per-interval sequential residual pre-pass eliminates scattered seeks in Stage 4.**
  New module [residual_pre_pass.py](../track_runner/residual_pre_pass.py) exposes
  `precompute_interval_residuals(reader, scene_transform, seed_start, seed_end, fwd_curve,
  bwd_curve, half_window, fps) -> dict`. Inside `solve_interval_analytical` in
  [interval_solver.py](../track_runner/interval_solver.py), immediately after
  `fit_interval_curves`, the worker walks its interval's frame range `[pad_lo..pad_hi]` in
  monotonic order via the existing `FrameReader.read_frame`. FrameReader's strategy-0
  sequential fast-path fires for every read after the first because the access pattern is
  strictly increasing -- no scattered seeks. The worker builds an in-memory dict keyed by
  `(frame_index, roi_tuple)` -> `(residual_uint8, validity_uint8)`. Both `propagate_forward_analytical`
  and `propagate_backward_analytical` in [velocity_model.py](../track_runner/velocity_model.py)
  accept a new `precomputed_store=None` parameter, forwarded into `_apply_blob_snap`, which in
  turn passes it into every `residual_motion.observe_blob_at` call.
  `observe_blob_at` in [residual_motion.py](../track_runner/residual_motion.py) gains
  a `precomputed_store=None` parameter; on a store hit it bypasses `compute_residual_for_frame`
  entirely, converts uint8 back to float32, and populates `residual_cache` so the other pass
  finds the result without re-checking. On a miss it falls through to the legacy reader path
  unchanged. The store is scoped to one `solve_interval_analytical` call and cleared on exit;
  no state escapes (C5). SCHEMA_VERSION is not bumped: the residuals are byte-identical to
  the on-the-fly path (uint8 quantization atol=1 verified by test). Baseline M1 numbers:
  cv2 scattered seek 2,599 ms median vs cv2 sequential 6.4 ms/frame. Plan:
  `~/.claude/plans/memoized-percolating-moler.md` (M3+M4). Test:
  `test_tr_residual_pre_pass.py` (4 tests:
  non-empty store, uint8 close to fresh reader path atol=1, None on reader=None, empty dict
  on degenerate interval).

- **Analyze-mode HTML diagnostic report (`--plot`) end-to-end functional (closes Patches 1-7 of analyze-mode HTML diagnostic report plan).** Running `analyze --plot` on a solved video now produces `tr_config/<stem>.encode_analysis.html`: a single self-contained HTML file with embedded JSON + inlined vanilla-JS canvas renderer, four panels (zoom-stability for zoom bouncing, zoom-multiple for `torso_height_multiple` compliance, camera motion, runner ground speed), interactive hover/drag-zoom/double-click-reset/series-toggle, graceful degradation when camera-motion data or scene transform is missing, and no external network dependency. Test count for `tests/test_tr_analyze_report.py`: 33. Plan: [ANALYZE_MODE_PLOTS.md](active_plans/ANALYZE_MODE_PLOTS.md). Patch 8 (this entry) and Patch 9 (integration test) close out the M3 milestone.

- **Documentation for analyze-mode HTML diagnostic report (Patch 7 of analyze-mode HTML diagnostic report plan).** [ANALYZE.md](modes/ANALYZE.md) gets a new "Diagnostic HTML report (--plot)" sub-section above the AUTO HELP block describing the artifact, the four panels, the graceful-degradation policy, and the interaction model. [TRACK_RUNNER_ANALYZE_AND_ENCODE.md](TRACK_RUNNER_ANALYZE_AND_ENCODE.md) gets a "Diagnostic report (--plot)" section with per-panel diagnostic interpretation guidance. [ENCODE_DESIGN.md](ENCODE_DESIGN.md) gets a one-sentence cross-link. AUTO HELP block in `docs/modes/ANALYZE.md` regenerated to include `-p, --plot`.

- **Patch 6 of analyze-mode HTML diagnostic report plan landed: end-to-end functional report.** [analyze_report.py](../track_runner/analyze_report.py) `_build_analyze_report_data` now wires the four panel builders into the `panels` list with the documented degradation matrix (4/3/2/0 panels by available inputs: full -> all four panels; motion present but scene transform missing -> 3 panels with no runner-speed; motion missing -> 2 panels with no camera-motion or runner-speed; trajectory missing -> 0 panels and warnings explain why). `_write_analyze_report_html` emits per-panel `<details>`+`<canvas>` sections only for panels that have data, each with a hardcoded interpretive `<p>` note keyed by panel id (`PANEL_NOTES` constant), an empty `.panel-controls` div populated by JS at render time, expanded inline CSS (warnings-section banner, panels styling, tooltip), and a real references section linking to `<stem>.encode_analysis.yaml` and `<stem>.yaml` (per-video config). [analyze_report_renderer.js](../data/js/analyze_report_renderer.js) is now a real vanilla-JS canvas renderer (~600 lines) implementing: hover-tooltips with frame, seconds (when fps known), and value of every visible series at that frame; drag-zoom on x-range synced across panels (mousedown -> drag -> mouseup commits); double-click reset to full frame range; per-panel checkbox-row series toggles with color swatches; null-as-gap rendering (no interpolation through nulls); twin-axis support with optional right axis (only when `panel.y_right_label` is non-null); reference lines drawn dashed below series (lower z-order); matplotlib-default-style palette `['#1f77b4', '#ff7f0e', '#2ca02c', '#d62728', '#9467bd']` indexed by series order; canvas backing-store sized to CSS pixels on first draw and on window resize. [cli.py](../track_runner/cli.py) `_mode_analyze` now invokes `analyze_report.write_analyze_report` when `args.write_plots` is set, with try/RuntimeError graceful degradation around `camera_motion.load_active_camera_motion_or_fail` (camera-motion data missing -> camera and speed panels skipped, warning surfaced both to stderr and into the HTML report's warnings section); the HTML output path is derived as `pathlib.Path(analysis_path).with_suffix('.html')`. New imports: `import sys`, `import pathlib`, `import analyze_report`. Five new structural tests in Group F of [test_tr_analyze_report.py](../tests/test_tr_analyze_report.py) cover the four-panel happy path, the three degradation cases (motion missing -> 2 panels; scene transform missing -> 3 panels), the no-external-URL contract across all three cases, and the series-length-equals-frames contract; pre-existing Group A tests `test_html_contains_required_structural_markers` and `test_canvas_ids_appear_in_spec_order` were updated to provide synthetic inputs since canvases are no longer rendered unconditionally. JS sanity-checked with `node --check`. Test count: 27 -> 32. Plan: `~/.claude/plans/groovy-singing-neumann.md`.

- **`_zoom_stability_panel_data`, `_zoom_multiple_panel_data`, `_camera_motion_panel_data` builders added to [analyze_report.py](../track_runner/analyze_report.py) (Patch 4 of analyze-mode HTML diagnostic report plan).** Zoom-stability emits raw `crop_h` + 9-frame nan-aware mean overlay (window matches the ~6-7 frame time constant of the 0.15 default `crop_post_smooth_size_strength` EMA per the 2026-05-02 changelog) plus `torso_h`/`torso_w` on the twin axis. Zoom-multiple emits the achieved `crop_h / torso_h` ratio with a dashed reference line at the configured `torso_height_multiple` from `tr_config/<stem>.yaml` (key nested at `config['processing']['torso_height_multiple']`; key absent or config None means reference line omitted). Camera-motion emits `hypot(dx, dy)` and `scale` from the active `MotionTrack`. Eight new tests cover constant-input pass-through, alternating-input attenuation (the canonical zoom-bouncing signal), panel-shape assertions, achieved-multiple constancy, reference-line presence/omission, and camera flat-magnitude. Module-level constant `ZOOM_BOUNCE_SMOOTH_WINDOW = 9` introduced as a sibling of `SPEED_SMOOTH_WINDOW = 5`. Per-frame extraction loops in zoom-stability and zoom-multiple are intentionally inlined; extract a shared helper if a third builder needs the pattern. Total tests in [test_tr_analyze_report.py](../tests/test_tr_analyze_report.py): 27.

- **`_runner_speed_per_frame`, `_running_mean_nan_aware`, `_runner_speed_panel_data`, and `_to_json_values` helpers added to [analyze_report.py](../track_runner/analyze_report.py) (Patch 5 of analyze-mode HTML diagnostic report plan).** The projection helper differences torso-center scene coordinates from `SceneTransform.pixel_to_scene`; missing frames record nan. The smoothing helper computes a centered nan-aware mean (used by both the runner-speed overlay and the zoom-stability panel) and asserts a positive window. The panel-data builder emits the runner-speed panel dict (raw + smoothed mean series) per the documented JSON schema; the smoothing window lives in a new module-level constant `SPEED_SMOOTH_WINDOW = 5` that drives the panel title's parenthetical, while the JSON series name `speed_5f_mean` stays hardcoded as a stable JS-renderer contract identifier. The module imports `numpy` only (no `math` import); distance is computed via `numpy.sqrt`. Eight new tests cover constant-velocity round-trip via x/y/w/h, constant-velocity round-trip via cx/cy (production path), single-nan tolerance, all-nan window (split out), missing-frame handling, panel-shape assertions, and y_right_label omission when fps is None or non-positive. Total tests in [test_tr_analyze_report.py](../tests/test_tr_analyze_report.py): 19.

- **New public helper `summarize_mismatches` in [tr_video_identity.py](../track_runner/tr_video_identity.py).** Formats the dict returned by `compare_video_identity` as multi-line human-readable output, labeling blocking and informational entries separately. Centralizes formatting for consistent wording across all callers and prevents drift in mismatch presentation.

- **`--debug-blob` CLI flag added (M0a plumbing).** New top-level flag `--debug-blob`
  (dest=`debug_blob`, `action='store_true'`, default off) added to
  [cli_args.py](../track_runner/cli_args.py) next to `-d/--debug`. The flag
  is wired through the full stack: `cli.py` `_run_solve` reads `args.debug_blob` via
  `getattr(args, "debug_blob", False)` and includes it in `solve_kwargs`;
  `interval_solver.solve_all_intervals` gains a `debug_blob: bool = False` parameter and
  passes it to `solve_queue.ExecutionContext`; `ExecutionContext` gains a
  `debug_blob: bool = False` field (docstring updated); `_dispatch_blob_pass` forwards it
  to `solver_workers.make_pool` via `getattr(context, "debug_blob", False)`;
  `make_pool` gains a `debug_blob: bool = False` parameter and includes it in `initargs`;
  `_worker_init` gains a `debug_blob: bool = False` parameter and stores it in the frozen
  `WorkerContext` dataclass (new field `debug_blob: bool = False`). The flag is OFF by
  default and has no behavioral effect until M0b instruments the worker code paths.
  Smoke test: `test_tr_debug_blob_flag.py`
  (3 tests: flag parses True, default is False, ExecutionContext round-trips the field).

- **M0b blob-pass instrumentation: per-strategy timing, per-pid exit summary, heartbeat thread, Stage 4 final summary.**
  All gated on `--debug-blob`; zero cost when the flag is off (one `if` check per call site; no string formatting, no timing, no prints on the hot path).
  (1) [frame_reader.py](../common_tools/frame_reader.py): module-level `_BLOB_DEBUG_STATS` accumulator; `enable_blob_debug()` / `disable_blob_debug()` / `get_blob_debug_summary()` public API. `read_frame` wraps each strategy in `time.perf_counter()` when enabled: prints `[blob][pid=NNN] read_frame fi=NNNNN strategy=<name> elapsed=X.Xms` on success; `STRATEGY_FAIL strategy=<name> retry=<next>` on failure; `CRITICAL strategy=sequential walked from N to N` on strategy 4 firing; `CRITICAL strategy=remux elapsed=N.Ns` on strategy 5.
  (2) [residual_motion.py](../track_runner/residual_motion.py): `_BLOB_DEBUG_RESIDUAL_ENABLED` flag; `enable_blob_debug_residual()` / `disable_blob_debug_residual()` / `get_residual_debug_summary()` public API. `observe_blob_at` prints `[blob][pid=NNN] observe_blob_at fi=NNNNN` at entry and increments `observe_calls`. `compute_residual_for_frame` (production path) times the full call, tracks per-call BGR cache hits/misses, and prints `[blob][pid=NNN] compute_residual fi=NNNNN neighbors=N elapsed=X.Xms cache_hits=A/B` at exit.
  (3) [solver_workers.py](../track_runner/solver_workers.py): `_worker_init` calls `enable_blob_debug()` and `enable_blob_debug_residual()` when `debug_blob` is True. `_worker_atexit` emits a per-pid `[blob][pid=NNN] WORKER EXIT SUMMARY` block with per-strategy call counts, median ms, failure counts, sequential/remux fire indicators, residual call counts, and BGR cache hit rate. Flags `*** CRITICAL ***` if strategy 4 or 5 fired.
  (4) [interval_solver.py](../track_runner/interval_solver.py): pool branch of `_dispatch_blob_pass` starts a 5-second `threading.Thread` heartbeat when `debug_blob` is on; each heartbeat tick prints `[blob][heartbeat] in_flight=N done=X/Y elapsed=M:SS intervals=[...]`. Thread is stopped in `try/finally` after the pool exits. After pool completes, prints `[blob] Stage 4 final summary` with dispatched/completed counts, total wall time, and P50/P95/max per-interval elapsed using `statistics.median`.
  Tests: `test_tr_debug_blob_instrumentation.py` (7 tests: enable/disable round-trips, summary key presence, copy semantics, default-off state).
  Plan: `~/.claude/plans/memoized-percolating-moler.md` (M0b).

- **Always-on `[blob] dispatch/complete` lines in Stage 4 pool branch.** The pool branch
  of `_dispatch_blob_pass` in [interval_solver.py](../track_runner/interval_solver.py)
  was silent until the first interval finished, giving the user no per-interval signal
  during a stalled Stage 4 run. Added always-on (not gated on `--debug-blob`) print lines
  matching the in-process branch: at submit time each task prints
  `[blob] dispatch pair_idx=N frames a-b (X frames)` and at future-completion each task
  prints `[blob] complete pair_idx=N elapsed=Y.YYs`. A `t_dispatch_by_pair` dict is
  populated at submit time and consumed at completion for the elapsed calculation. Both
  lines use `flush=True`. These are a permanent UX backstop regardless of debug flags.
  Plan: `~/.claude/plans/memoized-percolating-moler.md` (M0a).

- **`track_runner/analyze_report.py` skeleton landed (Patch 1 of analyze-mode HTML diagnostic
  report plan).** Module exposes public `write_analyze_report` and private helpers
  `_build_analyze_report_data`, `_write_analyze_report_html`, `_load_renderer_js`. The renderer
  loader resolves the repo root inline via `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` (with `cwd` anchored
  to `__file__.parent` so it works regardless of caller CWD, per docs/REPO_STYLE.md), captures
  git stderr, and raises a structured `FileNotFoundError` naming the asset path and the patch
  that creates it when the JS file is missing. M1 stub HTML emits the four `<canvas>` markers
  (`zoom-stability`, `zoom-multiple`, `camera-motion`, `runner-speed`), an embedded JSON
  `<script type="application/json">` block, and an inlined renderer JS `<script>` block. Real
  data builders, JS renderer, and CLI wiring follow in subsequent patches.
  Plan: `~/.claude/plans/groovy-singing-neumann.md`.

- **`-p`/`--plot` flag added to the `analyze` subparser; `data/js/analyze_report_renderer.js`
  stub created (Patch 2 of analyze-mode HTML diagnostic report plan).** The flag is on the
  analyze subparser only -- `solve --plot`, `refine --plot`, `encode --plot`, `target --plot`,
  and `setup --plot` all reject with an argparse error (`unrecognized arguments: --plot`).
  The JS asset is currently a no-op IIFE with a schema-comment header documenting the JSON
  contract the Patch-6 renderer will consume. The Patch-1 `_load_renderer_js()` now resolves
  successfully. Plan: `~/.claude/plans/groovy-singing-neumann.md`.

- **Four zoom-bounce assessment tools plus a recipe doc.** Investigation scaffolding for the post-2026-05-02 residual zoom-bounce question, scoped by `~/.claude/plans/declarative-shimmying-brooks.md` (manager plan). The user produces a post-encode tracked-movie corpus per variant; these tools turn the corpus into an evidence package without requiring any production crop-pipeline changes. (1) `find_zoom_hotspots.py`: Fourier-Mellin-driven hotspot finder that locates the top-N worst-bounce windows in a single encoded movie, writes a markdown report next to the input, and optionally extracts MP4 clips around each hotspot via `ffmpeg -c copy` (no re-encode). Reuses `compute_fft_log_polar` / `estimate_scale_fourier_mellin` / `build_edge_mask` from `assess_pixel_zoom.py` by import; does not duplicate that math. (2) `correlate_bounce_with_edge.py`: direct test of the Step 3.6 fit-to-source ratchet hypothesis. Pairs per-frame Fourier-Mellin scale signal from the encoded movie with per-frame runner-to-source-edge distance from the source video's solved trajectory (loaded via `state_io.load_torso_box_coords` + `interval_solver.stitch_trajectories` + `anchor_to_seeds` + `_apply_trajectory_erasure`, mirroring the encoder's transform sequence). Reports Spearman rho, scatter PNG with marginal histograms, and a per-quartile bounce-by-edge-gap markdown table. Positive correlation supports the ratchet hypothesis. (3) `spectrum_zoom_bounce.py`: FFT power spectrum of the per-frame log-scale series with a Hann window; plots on log-y with a dashed annotation at the EMA cutoff frequency `fps / (2 * pi * tau_frames)` so the eye can see whether residual energy sits above or below the EMA's expected attenuation band. Defaults `tau=6` to match the current `crop_post_smooth_size_strength=0.15`. (4) `rank_zoom_variants.py`: aggregates per-variant `pixel_zoom_comparison.csv` files (the user's corpus product) into a markdown ranking table with per-video deltas vs baseline plus a per-variant `win`/`tie`/`loss` verdict using a configurable threshold (default 10% improvement). Ranks on `bounce_rate_per_s` by default; `-H` flag flips for higher-is-better metrics like `valid_frame_fraction`. Recipe doc [ZOOM_BOUNCE_REVIEW_RECIPE.md](archive/ZOOM_BOUNCE_REVIEW_RECIPE.md) walks through the full workflow (assess + rank + hotspots + correlator + spectrum) and includes an `ffmpeg hstack/vstack` recipe for side-by-side visual review. Tests in `test_tr_zoom_bounce_tools.py` cover each tool with synthetic-signal fixtures (descending order, positive correlation, dominant-frequency tolerance, ranking sign behavior) per PYTHON_STYLE.md "good test" rules. All four tools are pyflakes clean, `--help` works on each, and the test module passes 5/5.

### Behavior or Interface Changes

- **WP4: Public API parameter rename `prior_intervals` -> `prior_solved_intervals` (C12.2 contract cleanup).** Durable on-disk artifacts in `tr_config/` are no longer labeled "cache" in user-facing code. The term "cache" is now reserved for in-memory ephemeral state. Per contract C12.2, two on-disk artifacts are durable solved-result stores, not cache: `<video>.track_runner.torso_box_coords.npz` (refine reads it; user cannot delete without re-solve) and `<video>.track_runner.camera_motion.npz` (refine reads it; user cannot delete without re-running Stage 1). Public API change: `interval_solver.solve_all_intervals` parameter renamed from `prior_intervals` to `prior_solved_intervals`. Callers: [cli.py](../track_runner/cli.py) `_run_solve`, [solve_queue.py](../track_runner/solve_queue.py) `plan_interval_work`. Docstrings in [interval_fingerprint.py](../track_runner/interval_fingerprint.py), [state_io.py](../track_runner/state_io.py), [camera_motion.py](../track_runner/camera_motion.py), and [interval_solver.py](../track_runner/interval_solver.py) updated to replace "cache" language with "store" or "artifact" (persistence sense only; in-memory caches kept their names). Updated user-facing docs in [TR_CONFIG_FILES.md](TR_CONFIG_FILES.md): "Cache semantics" section renamed to "Reuse semantics", torso_box_coords and camera_motion described as durable solved-result stores, new paragraph at the top clarifies the cache/artifact distinction (C12.2). All 196 tests pass; pyflakes clean on six production files.

### Fixes and Maintenance

- **FrameReader scattered-read fix: prefer `POS_FRAMES` over `POS_MSEC`.** Stage 4 blob promotion was stalling for 25+ minutes with 0/47 intervals completed on a 4K H.264 MOV after the `solver_workers` migration to `FrameReader`. Root cause: `read_frame` strategy 1 seeked via `cap.set(CAP_PROP_POS_MSEC, time_msec)`. On H.264 mp4/MOV containers, `POS_MSEC` typically forces a per-call keyframe re-seek; `POS_FRAMES` does not. The S1 validation on the bin plan only measured *sequential* reads (200 consecutive pair reads), where strategy 0's fast-path skips the `set(...)` entirely -- so the cliff was invisible until Stage 4, where `residual_motion.observe_blob_at` builds a temporal neighbor stack around frame `i` (i-N..i+N) per non-endpoint frame and the FWD/BWD passes hit those windows out of order. The legacy `track_runner/video_io.py::VideoReader` always seeked via `POS_FRAMES` (line 88), which is why the pre-migration blob path was fast on the same footage. Fix in [frame_reader.py](../common_tools/frame_reader.py): swap strategies 1 and 2 -- strategy 1 is now `POS_FRAMES`, strategy 2 is `POS_MSEC` (kept as fallback for codecs where `POS_FRAMES` does not honor random access). The strategy-0 sequential fast-path is unchanged. All 29 frame_reader tests still pass.

- **Defensive frame_count consistency checks in `load_torso_box_coords` and `load_motion_cache`.** Added internal-consistency validation to catch corrupt or partially-written files. In [state_io.py](../track_runner/state_io.py) `load_torso_box_coords`: after loading the manifest, if `video_identity` is present and contains `frame_count`, verify it is >= max(end_frame) from the manifest (solved intervals are a subset of the video). If frame_count < max_end_frame, raise RuntimeError with "file is corrupt or was trimmed". In [camera_motion.py](../track_runner/camera_motion.py) `load_motion_cache`: after loading motion arrays, if persisted `frame_count` disagrees with `len(dx)`, raise RuntimeError with "file is corrupt". Both checks are defensive and rare in practice (files are written atomically); they surface latent file I/O errors or partial-write corruption that would otherwise silently propagate to solve.

- **Per-interval stationary lock removed end-to-end from the propagator.** `track_runner/velocity_model.py` previously computed `is_stationary = displacement < 0.03 * left_h` between the two interval-bracketing seeds in `fit_interval_curves`, then (a) froze the raw Hermite curve at the left/right seed center and dimensions for the whole interval in `_compute_raw_pred_forward` / `_compute_raw_pred_backward`, (b) carried the flag as the 7th element of the raw-pred tuple, and (c) short-circuited blob-snap on stationary frames in `_apply_blob_snap` while stamping `stationary_lock` into every per-frame `snap_pred` dict. With Stage 3b (`solve_queue._solve_pre_race_interval`, contract clause C4) owning the entire pre-race range as a scene-anchored seed-averaged synthesis, the lock could only fire on POST-race intervals where two adjacent seeds happened to sit within 3% of the box height of each other -- effectively when the user placed two adjacent seeds at the same point. That case is rare and indistinguishable in output from a pure Hermite fit on the same two seeds, since Hermite on near-zero displacement is already effectively flat and the blob-snap velocity-floor branch (`v_pred_mag <= BLOB_SNAP_VELOCITY_FLOOR`) routes such frames through proximity-only acceptance. Deleted: the `is_stationary` computation and dict key in `fit_interval_curves`; the `if is_stationary:` freeze branch in both forward and backward raw-pred builders; the 7th tuple slot (raw-pred is now a 6-tuple `(frame_index, cx, cy, w, h, conf)`); the `or is_stat` short-circuit in the snap-pred loop and the `stationary_lock` key on both `snap_pred` output dicts. `tools/check_interval_blob_funnel.py` and `tools/check_prediction_divergence.py` updated to the new tuple shape; their `is_endpoint or is_stat` skip rule is now just `is_endpoint`. `docs/TR_CONFIG_FILES.md:172` per-frame-extras list drops `stationary_lock`. No `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump per contract C9: the persisted intervals JSON already dropped `stationary_lock` at write, so the on-disk schema is byte-stable. Behavior change for tight post-race seed pairs: instead of both endpoints' values being copied verbatim, the interval is filled by Hermite + proximity-gated blob-snap; expected divergence is sub-pixel. All comments referencing the lock are also stripped per the changelog-as-history rule -- the changelog records the mechanism so the code does not have to. Focused tests pass: `test_tr_velocity_model.py`, `test_tr_blob_snap.py`, `test_tr_stage4_parity.py`, `test_tr_race_phases.py`, `test_tr_scoring.py` (44/44), plus repo-wide `test_pyflakes_code_lint.py` (121/121). Plan: `~/.claude/plans/synchronous-cooking-giraffe.md`.

- **WP5: Documentation cleanup for config_hash, active.json, and debug_paths retirement.** Completed the final WP (docs-only) of the multi-patch series. Updated all user-facing and internal docs to reflect the post-WP1-WP4 code state. Changes: (1) [TR_CONFIG_FILES.md](TR_CONFIG_FILES.md) removed the `debug_paths.npz` file-map row and the entire "Debug interval paths NPZ (opt-in)" section including lifecycle and consumers subsections; updated `torso_box_coords` section schema_version from 2 to 10, dtype from float32 to uint16, and footprint from float to int; removed `config_hash` field from camera motion section and replaced "Lifecycle" with "Reuse semantics" describing single-canonical-file model; removed FAQ entries for debug_paths. (2) [TR_SCHEMA_VERSION_HISTORY.md](TR_SCHEMA_VERSION_HISTORY.md) verified v10 entry already present (added by WP2) with all required details (date, geometry-affecting, dtype change rationale, uint16 range notes, hard-cut policy). (3) [TR_CAMERA_MOTION_METHOD.md](TR_CAMERA_MOTION_METHOD.md) removed `config_hash` field description from save/load section; replaced hash-based staleness rules with motion_model comparison; updated contract C9 note to reflect motion_model-based invalidation. (4) [ENCODE_DESIGN.md](ENCODE_DESIGN.md) rewrote camera-motion identity section to describe canonical file + motion_model validation instead of active.json marker. (5) [SOLVE.md](modes/SOLVE.md) rewrote `--bin N` help to remove per-hash cache directory and active.json references. (6) [REFINE.md](modes/REFINE.md) rewrote camera-motion artifact loading to use canonical file with motion_model check instead of active.json marker. (7) Updated code comments in [cli_args.py](../track_runner/cli_args.py) (2 locations: comment + help text), [cli.py](../track_runner/cli.py) (1 location), [tr_paths.py](../track_runner/tr_paths.py) (2 locations in docstring) to replace "cache" with "artifact" or "store" for on-disk references; in-memory cache names retained. Grep passes: `config_hash\|active\.json` produces zero docs hits outside CHANGELOG (one contract reference remains in TRACK_RUNNER_CONTRACT.md documenting C12.3). `debug_paths` in docs/ produces six hits all in historical sections (CODE_ARCHITECTURE, FILE_STRUCTURE, TRACK_RUNNER_V3_SPEC, TR_SCHEMA_VERSION_HISTORY) documenting prior versions. Pyflakes: 125/125 passing across all lint files.

### Behavior or Interface Changes

- **`compare_video_identity` return type restructured: blocking vs informational mismatch categories per C12.3.** User policy: "renaming the file shouldn't matter"; cosmetic surface mismatches (basename, size_bytes, duration_s) are warnings only, never rejections. Data-shape mismatches (frame_count, width, height, fps) are blocking and catch real problems. Restructured [tr_video_identity.py](../track_runner/tr_video_identity.py) `compare_video_identity(stored, current)` to return a dict with two keys: `"blocking"` (frame_count, width, height, fps mismatch) and `"informational"` (basename, size_bytes, duration_s mismatch). Both are lists of strings; both empty means no mismatches. Added helper `summarize_mismatches(result: dict) -> str` to format the dict as human-readable multi-line output with labeled sections. Updated [cli.py](../track_runner/cli.py) `_check_identity_mismatch` to consume the dict and print via the helper; behavior unchanged (warnings only, never rejects). Module docstring clarified: video_identity is a metadata sanity check; blocking mismatches indicate data computed against a different video. New tests in [test_tr_video_identity.py](../tests/test_tr_video_identity.py) (15 tests) cover the categorization rules, formatting, empty results, and edge cases.

- **Per-frame torso-box coordinate arrays: float32 -> uint16 per C12.4.** Contract C12.4 requires
  "limit per frame content to only content that is needed." The per-frame arrays in
  `<video>.track_runner.torso_box_coords.npz` stored float32 coordinates despite fingerprint code
  rounding to 2 decimals and interval_solver producing pixel-snapped geometry. Schema version
  bumped from 9 to 10 (geometry-affecting). Writer [state_io.py](../track_runner/state_io.py)
  now rounds float coords to nearest int, clips to [0, 65535], and casts to uint16 before storage.
  Reader [state_io.py](../track_runner/state_io.py) reconstructs
  uint16 arrays as Python `int` (not numpy types) so downstream arithmetic does not overflow.
  Improved error message directs users to re-solve when rejecting old schemas.
  Tests verify round-trip rounding tolerance (+-1), uint16 on-disk dtype, and v9 rejection.
  Updated docs: [TR_SCHEMA_VERSION_HISTORY.md](TR_SCHEMA_VERSION_HISTORY.md) v10 entry.

### Removals and Deprecations

- **Schema v8 and v9 torso_box_coords artifacts no longer loadable (hard-cut on dtype change).** Per
  C12.4 and user policy ("everything else is gonna have to be recalculated anyway" when schema
  changes affect geometry), `SUPPORTED_ARTIFACT_SCHEMAS["torso_box_coords"]` hard-cuts from {8, 9}
  to {10} only. Videos with v8/v9 `.track_runner.torso_box_coords.npz` files will fail to load
  with a clear error directing users to re-solve. Rationale: the dtype change (float32 -> uint16)
  requires reconstruction path changes, and cached geometry from pre-uint16 schema does not survive
  round-trip without loss. `load_torso_box_coords` error message in
  [state_io.py](../track_runner/state_io.py) directs users to
  re-solve and specifies the required target schema version (v10). Updated docs:
  [TR_SCHEMA_VERSION_HISTORY.md](TR_SCHEMA_VERSION_HISTORY.md) v10 entry explains the policy.

- **config_hash retired from camera-motion module (C12.3 contract clean-up).** The per-hash
  subdirectory + `active.json` marker model for caching motion estimates is replaced with a
  single canonical file `<video>.track_runner.camera_motion.npz` per video. The motion_model
  is recorded as in-NPZ metadata. On load, mismatch with the current `camera.zoom_type`-derived
  motion_model triggers recompute and atomic overwrite. Deleted: (1) `config_hash` parameter
  from `save_motion_cache` ([camera_motion.py](../track_runner/camera_motion.py)).
  (2) `config_hash` array from the NPZ format; legacy persisted hashes are no longer loaded.
  (3) `_write_active_marker` function and all calls to it (lines 1087-1112, 1233-1236, 1292-1295).
  (4) Three `tr_paths` helpers: `camera_motion_cache_dir`, `camera_motion_cache_path_for_hash`,
  `camera_motion_active_marker_path` ([tr_paths.py](../track_runner/tr_paths.py)).
  (5) Unused imports: `hashlib`, `json` from camera_motion.py. Modified: (1)
  `load_motion_cache` signature changed from `expected_config_hash: str | None = None` to
  `expected_motion_model: str | None = None`; the staleness check now compares persisted
  motion_model against the current config-derived motion_model instead of comparing hash strings.
  (2) `load_active_camera_motion_or_fail` gains a `config: dict` parameter so it can compute the
  expected motion_model from the current configuration; old signature `(input_file: str)` becomes
  `(input_file: str, config: dict)`. Callers in [cli.py](../track_runner/cli.py)
  (refine and encode paths), [base_controller.py](../track_runner/ui/base_controller.py)
  (heat map loader) updated. (3) `precompute_camera_motion` drops the unused `cache_dir: str`
  parameter; the function now uses the canonical path directly via `tr_paths.default_camera_motion_path`.
  (4) Docstrings updated to reflect single-canonical-file model, motion_model-based staleness,
  and no per-hash directory. Stale comments about `active.json` in cli.py and base_controller.py
  are cleaned up. Tests adjusted: `test_stale_config_hash_treated_as_miss` renamed and
  rewritten as `test_stale_motion_model_treated_as_miss`; test config-fingerprint tests
  (`test_config_fingerprint_*`) removed since the fingerprint computation itself no longer exists.
  Tests still cover round-trip invariants, missing-file, unknown-model, and required-array validation.
  All camera_motion tests pass (14/14); all lint tests pass (125+ files). Rationale (C12.3):
  The hash-per-config approach fragmented caches by estimator type, bin_factor, and processed
  geometry; the canonical-file model is simpler (one cache per video per motion_model), fully
  backward-compatible (motion_model mismatch triggers recompute), and non-destructive (old caches
  are never deleted, just not used). Refine's contract ("load the exact cache solve bound")
  remains upheld via the motion_model persistence; stale configs are caught on read without
  requiring a separate marker file. Follow-up: `docs/TR_CAMERA_MOTION_METHOD.md`,
  `docs/TR_CONFIG_FILES.md`, `docs/ENCODE_DESIGN.md`, `docs/modes/SOLVE.md`, and
  `docs/modes/REFINE.md` still describe the retired `config_hash` and `active.json`
  marker as live behavior; these are flagged for the WP5 doc-cleanup patch in
  `~/.claude/plans/parsed-honking-curry.md`. Code review for this WP also pulled the
  duplicated config-to-motion_model derivation out into a private helper
  `_motion_model_from_config(config: dict) -> str` (camera_motion.py, near
  `_estimator_type_to_model`) so `load_active_camera_motion_or_fail` and
  `precompute_camera_motion` cannot drift on the rule. Added behavioral test
  `test_bin_factor_does_not_invalidate_camera_motion_cache` in
  [test_tr_camera_motion_bin.py](../tests/test_tr_camera_motion_bin.py)
  to lock in the new policy that bin and processed-geometry are not part of the
  staleness check.

### Developer Tests and Notes

- **`tests/test_tr_analyze_report.py` added: M1 stub coverage for analyze-mode HTML report (Patch 3 of analyze-mode HTML diagnostic report plan).** Nine tests covering HTML structural markers (canvas IDs in spec order, JSON script block, references section, video-stem in h1), JSON-parse roundtrip on the embedded data block, no-external-URL contract, ASCII-only output, warnings-section omission/presence/HTML-escaping. Tests monkeypatch `_load_renderer_js` so they do not depend on the on-disk asset.

### Fixes and Maintenance

- **`--debug-blob` flag relocated from top-level parser to `solve` and `refine` subparsers.** First user invocation of `solve --upgrade --bin 4 --debug-blob` failed with `error: unrecognized arguments: --debug-blob` because argparse subparsers consume all post-subcommand args and reject unknown flags. The original M0a wired the flag onto the top-level parser at the same position as `-d/--debug` (so `--debug-blob solve ...` worked), but every existing subcommand-specific flag in this repo (`--bin`, `--upgrade`, `--full`, `--keep`, etc.) lives on the subparser so users naturally type the diagnostic flag after `solve`. New helper `_add_debug_blob_arg(parser)` in [cli_args.py](../track_runner/cli_args.py) registers the flag once; both `solve_parser` and `refine_parser` call it. Top-level registration removed; `set_defaults(debug_blob=False)` removed from the top-level parser. Smoke test `test_tr_debug_blob_flag.py` updated to put `--debug-blob` after the subcommand and added a `test_debug_blob_flag_parses_true_on_refine` mirror; 11/11 pass.

- **M3+M4 pre-pass cache-population fix: eliminate residual scattered seeks inside the per-worker pre-pass.** First M3+M4 implementation showed Stage 4 still doing 292 `seek_frame` strategy calls per run despite the architectural fix (~50 per worker per interval). Root cause: two bugs in [residual_pre_pass.py](../track_runner/residual_pre_pass.py) `precompute_interval_residuals`. (1) Sliding-window eviction during the sequential walk dropped BGR entries below `fi - half_window - 2`, so by the time the residual-computation loop ran, only the last ~11 frames near `pad_hi` were in cache; every interval frame's neighbors fell through to `reader.read_frame` as scattered seeks. Fix: removed eviction; the entire `pad_lo..pad_hi` BGR range stays in `frame_cache` until the function returns. Memory budget per worker per interval: ~600 MB at bin=4 for a 377-frame interval (well under the 36 GB system budget for 7 concurrent workers). (2) The pre-pass populated only `frame_cache[("bgr", fi)]` (BGR uint8); `_read_gray_frame` in `compute_residual_for_frame` uses integer key `frame_cache[fi]` for grayscale-float32. Every center-frame gray read missed the cache and hit `reader.read_frame` as a scattered seek. Fix: pre-convert each BGR frame to gray-float32 during the walk and store at the integer key. After both fixes the live `--debug-blob` instrumentation shows compute_residual cache hit rate at 16/16 (100%) per call and Stage 4 wall time dropped from "never completes" to 2:52 wall on the user's iPhone HEVC HDR file (58 intervals, P50=65.8 s, P95=135.1 s, max=138.8 s). Plan: `~/.claude/plans/memoized-percolating-moler.md` M3+M4 verification gate.

- **Heartbeat thread snapshots dispatch/complete dicts to avoid concurrent-mutation races.** Found by post-implementation bug-hunt review. The `--debug-blob` heartbeat thread in [interval_solver.py](../track_runner/interval_solver.py) iterated `t_dispatch_by_pair` while the main thread concurrently wrote to it on each task submit; in CPython, `dict.__setitem__` during `dict.__iter__` can raise `RuntimeError: dictionary changed size during iteration` (rare at 5-second intervals but possible under load). Same race for `t_complete_by_pair` (writes at completion vs `in` membership reads in heartbeat). Fix: snapshot both dicts at the top of the heartbeat loop body via `dict(dispatch_map)` and `dict(complete_map)` before iterating. The heartbeat thread is daemon=True so a crash there was silent (printing just stops); the fix preserves the diagnostic output reliability through full Stage 4 runs.

- **M2 trailing call-site fixes in `compute_heat_map_roi` consumers.** The M2 stride migration removed `window_seconds` from `compute_heat_map_roi`, but three call sites still passed it: [heat_map_overlay.py](../track_runner/ui/heat_map_overlay.py) `_compute_and_display`, the patched `_stub_residual` in [test_tr_residual_motion_bin.py](../tests/test_tr_residual_motion_bin.py), and the direct call in [test_tr_residual_heat_map.py](../tests/test_tr_residual_heat_map.py) `test_facade_output_is_roi_scoped_not_full_frame`. All three updated to drop the kwarg; the `_stub_residual` signature swapped `window_seconds=None` for `stride=None` to match the production signature post-M2. After the fix repo-wide `tests/test_tr_*` pass 562/562.
